From Files to Cited Answers in One API Call: Managed Ingestion and SearchDocuments in Agent Retrieval

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A guide to the new managed ingestion and SearchDocuments features in Agent Retrieval, for searching PDFs, Office files, and videos without writing an ingestion pipeline.

Before an agent can answer a question about your PDFs, someone has to build the pipeline: parse every file, split it into chunks, embed each chunk, write the chunks to a vector database, and then learn that database's schema to query it. For video it gets harder: transcode the files, cut them into clips, embed the frames, and keep track of which second of which video each clip came from.

Most of that code has nothing to do with your application. Two features recently added to Agent Retrieval (formerly Vector Search 2.0), now in Public Preview, take that work off your hands. Managed ingestion builds the pipeline for you: point a data store at files in Cloud Storage, and the service parses, segments, and embeds them with Gemini Embedding 2. SearchDocuments, the second new feature, then searches the data store with one API call and returns the matching passages with their page numbers or timestamps. In our tests, 18 Alphabet investor PDFs went from a public Cloud Storage bucket to searchable, page cited results in about 9 minutes, and 19 minutes of video became searchable by both visuals and spoken audio in 0.3 to 0.5 seconds per query, with no parsing, chunking, transcription, or embedding code.

This guide covers how to create a data store, import documents and videos, and search them with filters, reranking, and audio-aware video search.

Pairing managed ingestion with SearchDocuments gives you several immediate benefits:

Agent Retrieval Collections already give you semantic, text, and hybrid search over your own data objects. But you have to produce those data objects yourself, and to query them you need to know how they are stored. Here is what changes when you let a data store do the work:

The backing Collection is still a regular Agent Retrieval Collection, so you can query it directly whenever you need full control.

A data store holds your files and the configuration that controls how they are parsed and segmented. The data store and SearchDocuments APIs are currently served over REST at v1beta, so the examples use an authorized session from google-auth:

Two segmentation modes are available. Chunk segmentation (the default) splits text into chunks of 100 to 500 tokens and works with layout parsing, which also detects tables and headings. Page segmentation produces one segment per page. For paginated documents such as PDFs, both modes record page numbers (startPage and endPage) so you can cite pages or filter by page range: page segmentation returns a single page per segment, while chunk segmentation returns the page range each chunk spans.

Point an import at your files with URI patterns such as gs://my-bucket/reports/*.pdf, or with a JSONL manifest when you want to set your own document IDs and attach metadata:

The import runs as a long-running operation. Re-importing the same files updates existing documents instead of duplicating them, and files whose content didn't change are skipped.

SearchDocuments needs only the data store and a text query:

Each result is a segment of a document, with the text, the page, and the source file:

Pass filter to search only the segments that match, using the same paths you read back in results:

You can also pass document_ids to search only inside specific files, and output_fields.metadata to return each document's metadata with its results. In our test, searching "What did the CEO say about AI?" with the page-1 filter returned the CEO quotes from the headline page of five different earnings releases.

For "How many employees does Alphabet have?", plain hybrid search returned two balance sheet pages among the top five, which don't mention employees at all. With reranking on, every top result reported the headcount: the annual report's "Alphabet had 190,234 employees" and the "Number of employees" row of the quarterly supplemental tables.

Video works the same way: create a data store with document_type: VIDEO, import the files, and call SearchDocuments. The service transcodes each video, splits it into fixed windows, and embeds each window:

Each result carries the moment it matched:

We imported five Google sample videos (about 19 minutes in total) and searched them with scene descriptions. "Cucumbers being sorted by a machine" returned 03:20-03:40 of the machine learning video, where a farm's sorting machine grades cucumbers, without any tags or captions. Searches returned in 0.3 to 0.5 seconds.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

By default (audio_mode: DISABLED), only the frames are embedded, so a query matches what is shown. With ENABLED, the first audio track is embedded together with the frames. We imported the same videos into two data stores and asked both about statements from a keynote, spoken while the picture shows the speaker:

Only the audio-enabled data store found the right moments.

The rule of thumb: If your data is files, let a data store ingest them and search with SearchDocuments. Drop down to the Collection only when you need control the data store doesn't give you.

Managed ingestion doesn't change what good retrieval needs: well-segmented content, good embeddings, and the right filters. What changes is who builds it. Instead of maintaining parsers, chunkers, embedding jobs, and a schema, you point a data store at a bucket and get back segments that are ready to cite.

For agents, that means a retrieval tool that returns where each answer came from, a page in a PDF or a moment in a video, with one API call.

Try it yourself: the document notebook ingests 18 Alphabet investor PDFs and walks through filters and reranking, and the video notebook searches five sample videos with an inline player and compares audio modes.

Building a 160-Page Regional-Language Exam Prep Site with Astro 5, Tailwind v4, and Firebase Hosting

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Most Indian exam-preparation websites targeting tier-2 and tier-3 towns suffer from the same problem: heavy client-side JavaScript bundles, intrusive ad networks, slow hydration on budget Android phones, and broken regional-language (Devanagari/Marathi) typography.

While building Jijaee — an independent Marathi preparation platform and Android app for Maharashtra Police Bharti aspirants — we wanted the web companion to load almost instantly on 4G mobile data while still supporting 160+ static pages, interactive practice quizzes, a physical-test score calculator, and district-wise cutoff datasets.

Here is the architecture we chose and five real-world engineering bugs we ran into (and fixed) along the way.

With Astro 5 (output: "static") + Tailwind CSS v4, the entire 161-page site builds in ~6–9 seconds, ships almost zero client JS by default (only tiny inline scripts for interactive quizzes and calculators), and deploys cleanly to Firebase Hosting with aggressive CDN caching (Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable on /_astro/**).

One subtle technical SEO bug bit us early:

That created a silent redirect mismatch across all 150+ URLs: Googlebot read /cutoff/ from sitemap-0.xml, requested it, and hit a 301 redirect from Firebase Hosting to /cutoff.

After rebuilding, every single URL in sitemap-0.xml, , and internal tags matched 200 OK with zero redirect hops.

Our Marathi blog posts and daily current-affairs pages store dates as YYYY-MM-DD. When we built the static site from a machine in America/New_York (UTC-5), calling new Date('2026-11-17') parsed it as UTC midnight — which shifted backward to November 16 in local time!

Instead of relying on the build host's system timezone, we parsed calendar dates directly and forced Asia/Kolkata (+05:30) for full timestamps:

Simple, deterministic, and completely immune to whether CI runs in UTC, EST, or IST.

Our question bank powers SSR-rendered previews (so users and search engines see real practice questions without logging in). Auditing the rendered HTML surfaced three messy real-world data issues:

Maharashtra Police recruitment is decentralized — every district police unit publishes its own selection and cutoff PDFs on separate .gov.in portals. Many third-party exam blogs mistakenly mix 50-mark physical ground cutoffs with 100-mark written exam marks or 150-mark final merit totals.

To fix this on our Maharashtra Police Bharti district cutoff hub, we wrote Python scripts using PyMuPDF (fitz) to parse official selection lists and 1:10 ground shortlist PDFs directly from .gov.in portals, separating:

One edge case in official PDF tables: rows where the optional NCC Marks column is blank collapse from 4 numeric tokens (Physical, Written, NCC, Total) to 3 tokens (Physical, Written, Total). Validating total === physical + written + ncc caught that immediately and prevented missing ~35% of candidates during extraction.

If you're building a regional-language web app or static content platform with Astro, happy to answer questions in the comments!

She learned this week that her Impala will not pass inspection due to a rusted frame. She has a week to buy a new car, so she can't dawdle and is probably limited to what she can find locally.

She is dead set on a sedan and has not interest in any SUV. I think that is sensible.

Went out looking today. Two she liked:

1) 2020 Toyota Camry Hybrid, 26K miles, CPO, asking price around $24,500 (I can't remember exactly). At a Toyota dealer. She did not set out to look for a hybrid, but neither would she object. The higher MPG would be nice, but I suppose the hybrid system adds some complexity. Her trusted mechanics are at a very conveniently-located Chevy dealer. They could do the basics but it's a safe assumption they would not work on the Toyota hybrid system.

2) 2022 Honda Accord, 52K miles, asking price around $23,000. In my estimation, the Accord is the closest match to Impala size/shape/driving feel that we are likely to find nearby and in time. The instrument panel display was clean and uncluttered. Even this would be a fuel-economy improvement over her 2014 Impala. It is probably easily serviceable just about anywhere---your basic car.

Opinions?
I suggest attempting to research whether there are any known issues with either specific model. I would guess that given all else being equal, the Toyota is a better value due simply to whatever warranty extension(?) the CPO gets you plus the 26k fewer miles. What is the price for both models new, similarly optioned?

The CFTC opens the door to Bitcoin reserves at US exchanges with a 20% catch

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CFTC guidance lets exchanges count Bitcoin toward operating reserves, exposing annual funding buffers to crypto price swings.

On Nov. 23, Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) staff said US futures exchanges and derivatives clearinghouses may count Bitcoin, Ethereum, and qualifying payment stablecoins toward a rolling year of operating costs after applying valuation discounts.

A hypothetical selloff shows how a firm can lose part of that buffer while its liquid assets still cover six months of expenses.

The agency's announcement described the letter as staff guidance. Existing rules remain in force, and the letter offers no assurance that staff would decline to recommend enforcement.

Under the rules for designated contract markets and derivatives clearing organizations, exchanges and clearinghouses need resources to cover at least one year of projected operating costs on a rolling basis.

Within that annual pool, a separate liquidity test calls for enough unencumbered cash or highly liquid securities to cover at least six months of expenses.

The rules allow committed credit lines or similar facilities to make up a liquidity shortfall under certain conditions. The crypto assets in the advisory do not count toward that six-month test.

For the annual calculation, staff says they would raise no objection to the approach in the advisory, which includes a minimum valuation discount of 20% for Bitcoin and Ethereum and a 2% discount for qualifying payment stablecoins.

These discounts, known as haircuts, apply to current market value, so the amount a firm can count moves with the price.

Take a hypothetical exchange with $12 million in annual costs spread evenly across the year, $6 million in unencumbered cash or other eligible liquid assets, and a Bitcoin position worth $7.5 million.

At a fixed 20% haircut, the Bitcoin counts for $6 million. Together, the liquid assets and the discounted Bitcoin position count for $12 million. That matches the annual cost estimate and leaves the exchange with zero headroom.

A 25% drop in the Bitcoin price reduces the position to $5.6 million before the haircut and $4.5 million after. Once the position is valued at the lower price, the firm has $10.5 million in counted resources, $1.5 million below the annual cost estimate.

The $6 million in eligible liquid assets still covers the six-month floor.

A hypothetical futures exchange holds $7.5 million in Bitcoin and applies a fixed 20% valuation discount.

The exchange still covers six months with liquid assets. Its annual buffer falls short.

Projected costs and the haircut stay fixed, and the exchange raises no new funds. It starts with zero headroom above projected annual costs.

Hypothetical, in US dollars. Annual costs are $12 million, spread evenly. Eligible liquid assets stay at $6 million. Apply a fixed 20% haircut to $7.5 million in Bitcoin, then to $5.625 million after a 25% price drop. Counted Bitcoin falls from $6 million to $4.5 million. Subtract total counted resources of $10.5 million from projected annual costs to obtain the $1.5 million shortfall.

Projected costs and the haircut stay fixed, and the exchange raises no new funds. It starts with zero headroom above projected annual costs.

Staff also flag the risk that operating losses could coincide with falling crypto prices. In that case, a firm's reserve could lose value just as it needs more resources to absorb those losses.

At the stated benchmarks, $1 million worth of Bitcoin or Ethereum counts as $800,000, while $1 million in qualifying payment stablecoins counts as $980,000. The smaller discount for payment stablecoins comes with conditions on which tokens qualify.

Before the GENIUS Act takes effect, staff limit the stablecoin treatment to dollar-denominated tokens from a state-regulated money transmitter or trust company, or a national trust bank.

The issuer must back the tokens with reserves limited to cash, US Treasuries, or overnight Treasury repurchase agreements, held directly or indirectly.

Monthly attestations must cover what the reserves hold and whether their fair value matches the amount in circulation.

Once the law takes effect, the advisory applies its definition and requires a permitted issuer or a foreign issuer that meets the law's applicable conditions.

Customer margin is posted against trading positions, and clearinghouses separately set aside resources to cover clearing-member defaults. The clearinghouse rule bars them from counting the same resources toward both default coverage and operating costs.

The commission registered Backpack Clearing, formerly TLD Clearing Inc., on Nov. 13. Its registration order permits it to clear margined futures as well as fully collateralized futures, options on futures, and swaps.

Those records do not disclose what Backpack Clearing holds for operating costs or whether it uses the staff guidance.

Private Edtech Large File Browser Upload: Multipart Presigned Access Control

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Short answer: use a small application control plane to authorize a private upload, create a multipart session, and issue short-lived presigned URLs for each part. Let the browser send bytes directly to object storage, then let the control plane complete or abort the session. This keeps access control in the application without making the application server carry the file.

That is the least complex design that still gives an edtech product a useful recovery story for a large lecture recording, assignment archive, or dataset. It also makes the important failure visible: an upload is a state machine, not a single request.

Before choosing an SDK or storage service, decide which side of the boundary owns each fact. The application owns identity, policy, and the upload record. Storage owns part transfer and assembly. This decision survives a provider change; a provider-specific URL format does not.

The production scenario is bounded and familiar: a student selects a private lecture recording, the browser uploads every part, and the UI shows success. A later download request still needs a fresh authorization decision. If the application treats the upload URL as proof of read access, or if it marks the row complete before storage confirms completion, the system has confused delivery with authorization. I've seen enough missed jobs and duplicate deliveries to distrust a green progress bar on its own.

The invariant is simple: the application owns identity and state; object storage owns byte transfer. Keep those responsibilities separate and a retry remains a retry instead of a second delivery. It sounds obvious. Production has a way of making obvious rules expensive.

The browser should never decide who may read or write an object. It asks the application for an upload session. The application authenticates the user, validates the declared size and content type, chooses an object key, and records the owner, expiry, expected part count, and status before returning URLs.

The browser uploads each numbered part directly to storage. It sends the resulting part identifier back to the application. The application verifies that the part belongs to the session and, only after all expected parts are accounted for, completes the upload. Download access is a separate decision: issue a short-lived presigned GET only after the user is allowed to see that object.

That session row should contain more than an object key. Persist the authenticated owner, expected byte size, part size, expected part count, storage upload ID, application deadline, and terminal status. Those fields let a reconciler distinguish a slow transfer from a client that vanished. They also let support answer the only question that matters during an incident: who was allowed to finish this upload, and what did the system believe had happened?

Here is the control-plane shape in Go. The storage client is intentionally behind the workflow; the HTTP handlers around it should enforce authentication, authorization, size limits, and origin policy.

The example omits one import for readability of the workflow: types is github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2/service/s3/types. In a real service, persist the session before returning any presigned URL, and generate those URLs from the same bucket, key, upload ID, and part numbers. The browser may retry one part, but a retry should replace that part's recorded ETag. It should not create a second logical part.

One more rule matters. Completion must be idempotent. Store the terminal object metadata and return it when the same client request is repeated. A timeout after storage completes is normal network ambiguity; treating the retry as a new upload creates the duplicate-delivery incident the design was meant to avoid.

Three requests commonly arrive in an inconvenient order: a part retry, a user cancellation, and completion. Define the allowed transitions in the database: pending -> uploading -> complete, or pending/uploading -> aborted. A terminal row stays terminal. Completion checks ownership, session expiry, the exact part set, and the current status in one transaction before calling storage.

That last detail deserves emphasis. A transaction cannot roll back a storage completion. Use a small reconciliation job for the uncertain window: if storage reports completion but the application timed out before recording it, inspect the session and repair the row. If cancellation wins, call abort and mark the row aborted. The background job should retry an abort and report the attempt; it should not silently delete application evidence.

The hard case is a timeout after the storage service has accepted the final request. The browser sees no response, retries, and may race a support-triggered cancellation. A useful repair record captures the session ID, the last known part list, the completion attempt, the cancellation attempt, and the final storage observation. The reconciler then applies one rule: a confirmed completed object is recorded as complete, an unconfirmed session is left visible for another check, and an explicitly aborted session is never reopened by a late browser callback. That is more state than a one-request upload, but it is the state that explains why a private student file is downloadable, still uploading, or intentionally gone.

I have been paged for missed jobs and duplicate deliveries, so I put this sequence in the runbook, not in a comment nobody reads. The useful alerts are sessions past expiry, completion requests failing repeatedly, and objects whose application status does not match the recorded upload result. Your mileage may vary on thresholds; measure transfer duration and cancellation behavior before choosing them.

Storage lifecycle cleanup is a backstop. It is not a substitute for the cancel path, especially when users expect an abandoned upload to stop consuming resources quickly. Keep the application deadline shorter than the storage lifecycle window and make the difference explicit in operations documentation.

For private student uploads, multipart is a good fit when files are large enough that retrying from zero is painful and the team can own a session table. It is not suitable when the product only accepts small files, cannot run cleanup, or needs a single atomic request with no client-side coordination. Stick with a single presigned PUT for the small-file path; keep the multipart path for the failure mode it actually improves.

Access control also has a boundary that is easy to miss. A presigned URL is a bearer capability for its lifetime. Keep its lifetime short, bind it to one object and one part, avoid putting sensitive data in the key, and never use an upload URL as a download URL. For downloads, authorize the current user at request time and issue a new read URL. Private means the application still makes the decision.

Test the ugly cases first: an out-of-order part list, a duplicate part number, one part retried with a new ETag, an expired session, a completion retry after a client timeout, cancellation during completion, and a user attempting to finish another user's session. Verify that every rejected request leaves a diagnosable status.

The browser needs the storage origin's CORS policy to allow the methods and headers used by the presigned requests. The server needs request limits and content validation. The object key should be unguessable, but that is not authorization. Observability should include a session ID, user ID, object key hash, part count, and terminal transition, while avoiding raw bearer URLs in logs.

The postmortem question is simple: could the team tell which state the upload was in when the browser disappeared? If the answer is no, adding another retry loop will not fix the system. Record the state first.

Read a model's mistakes in Excel and a Windows preview

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a bike rental experiment, a random forest missed eight fewer regular-demand cases than a simple calendar rule. It also produced 28 more false alarms for that class. I wanted a Windows preview that makes those trade-offs visible to someone using Excel. Japanese original

Use Python with Tcl/Tk and install DIY AutoML in .venv. From the repository root, run this PowerShell command to open the preview with Iris selected. The preview currently uses Japanese labels, which are shown alongside their English meanings below. Setup and complete walkthrough

Model comparison requires finite numeric features, complete values, and recorded class labels. Before inspection, select IDでまとめる (Group by ID) to keep a customer's repeated rows together, or 日付順で分ける (Split by date) to evaluate later observations. Input and split options (Japanese)

On Iris, the result showed 30 evaluated, 29 correct, and one error. 評価Excelを開く (Open evaluation workbook) opens bp_report.xlsx. Start with まず読む (Start here) for the totals and ラベル別 (By outcome) for missed cases and false alarms. The model comparison bar chart under グラフを見る… (View charts) shows training-side validation scores. Holdout scores appear on the results screen and in the workbook. How to read the workbook

English summary redrawn from the saved Iris example workbook. Original workbook and evaluation conditions (Japanese)

Date-ordered evaluations add a monthly holdout chart. In Bike Sharing, I defined 200 or more rentals in a recorded hour as busy, and anything below that as regular. I held out the latest fifth of observed dates, which gave 582 rows from August 7 to December 31, 2012. I compared the selected random forest with a simple rule that predicts the most frequent training label for the same day of the week and hour. Bike Sharing dataset · Data preparation and split (Japanese)

The overall majority rule always predicts the most frequent label in the training period. Macro F1 gives the busy and regular classes equal weight.

Across the full period, the weekday-and-hour rule scored 0.656 macro F1 and the random forest scored 0.638. The random forest caught 80 of the 109 regular cases, and the rule caught 72. The forest missed 29 regular cases versus 37 for the rule. It also labeled 143 busy cases as regular, compared with 115 for the rule. The model scored higher in August and September, and the rule scored higher from October through December. These numbers describe the held-out period. Intervals that account for time dependence remain a research task. In Excel, the simple-rule comparison, monthly breakdown, and monthly breakdown by outcome let you go back to the counts for each period. Monthly comparison details (Japanese)

The charts use the saved evaluation tables. You can also open those worksheets in a charting tool or export them as CSV. For example, use Model and F1 from SL_CV_Mean to plot the training-side validation scores. Charting examples in the development notes

One idea is to put PyGWalker inside Streamlit so a user can choose the chart type, axes, and series from the saved tables. In the local test with Python 3.14.7 and PyGWalker 0.5.0.1, an Iris evaluation table produced an HTML file for local viewing. PyGWalker Streamlit documentation · Prototype log (Japanese)

The proposed Windows workflow uses an initial batch file on a PC with Python already installed. The batch would create a charting environment, get the dependencies over the network, and start the viewer. After that, a button in the preview could reuse the environment and open the current evaluation in a local browser. The Streamlit integration, initial setup, and button launch still need to be tested together. Charting roadmap (Japanese)

That wraps up the classification examples. The next article uses the same chronological holdout idea to check predictions of future observations.

AI assistance was used to adapt the project's development notes and experiment records into this English article.

greenServeFE programming language (open-source project)

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I’ve been working on an open-source programming language called greenServeFE. It is a programming language with its own syntax, compiler, virtual machine, and native modules, written primarily in C.

The project started with a simple idea: I wanted to build something of my own rather than always depending on existing programming languages and frameworks. I was interested in understanding how programming languages actually work behind the scenes. How does a language read source code? How does it understand expressions and functions? How does it execute instructions? How can it interact with the operating system?

Instead of just reading about these concepts, I decided to build them.

Over time, greenServeFE grew beyond a small language experiment. I developed its lexer, parser, compiler, bytecode system, and virtual machine. I also started building native C modules that extend what programs written in the language can do.

One of the most interesting parts of the project has been the HTTP server module. My goal was to let developers build server-side applications using greenServeFE without needing Node.js or another runtime for the application itself.

The server module supports HTTP routes, request and response objects, middleware, static files, HTML responses, JSON responses, templates, and other server capabilities. I have also been working on improving its underlying implementation.

I wanted greenServeFE to be more than a syntax demonstration. I wanted people to be able to write actual programs with it.

I decided to make the project open-source because I want other developers to explore the implementation, understand how it works, report problems, and contribute ideas. I also want the source code to be useful to people who are interested in learning how programming languages and virtual machines are built.

At the beginning, I expected the hardest part would be designing the language syntax and getting the compiler to work.

I thought that once the parser and virtual machine were working, adding features would become relatively straightforward. I expected to write a few native modules, create examples, and gradually make the language more capable.

But developing a programming language is different from writing an ordinary application.

Every feature introduces new interactions with the existing implementation. A change in the parser can affect compilation. A compiler change can affect bytecode execution. A change in the runtime can affect native modules. Even something that looks simple from the user's perspective can require changes across several parts of the system.

I also expected that documenting the language would be relatively easy. In reality, keeping documentation, examples, module APIs, and actual implementation behavior consistent is another challenge.

One experience that made this especially clear was developing the HTTP server module.

I could register routes and start the server, but that did not necessarily mean every request-handling feature worked as expected.

For example, I created an HTML form that submitted customer and product information to a route. The page loaded, the form submitted, and the route executed. However, the values received by the handler were null.

At first, it looked like a small problem with accessing the request object. But it made me realize that I needed to understand the actual server implementation and its request-parsing behavior rather than assume that an API worked in the same way as a familiar framework.

I started comparing the documentation, examples, and C implementation. I found differences between what was documented and what the implementation supported. I also had to consider whether the installed native module matched the source code I was examining.

That experience reminded me that a programming language is not just its syntax. The runtime and native modules are equally important.

I have approached greenServeFE by building and testing individual components rather than trying to implement everything at once.

I started with the language itself: lexical analysis, parsing, compilation, and bytecode execution. I then expanded the language with functions, variables, control flow, arrays, objects, and other runtime features.

After that, I began working on native modules written in C. These modules allow greenServeFE programs to use functionality that would otherwise require implementing everything inside the language.

I have worked on modules for mathematics, JSON, computation, validation, strings, file operations, cryptography, and other utilities.

The HTTP server module has been a particularly interesting challenge because it connects the language to real network requests. It requires handling routes, request data, responses, static files, and server configuration.

I have also spent time improving the developer experience. I want installation to be straightforward, examples to be understandable, and documentation to explain the actual APIs instead of presenting features that do not exist in the implementation.

Not everything has worked on the first attempt. Some syntax produced parser errors. Some module functions behaved differently from what I expected. Some features required changes to the underlying C implementation.

I have also had to deal with development-environment limitations. I primarily work on Windows, while much of the building and testing happens in Linux cloud environments. Network reliability and limited computing resources sometimes make it difficult to follow the development workflow I originally planned.

One thing I have learned is to avoid treating a successful build as proof that a feature is complete. I need to test the actual behavior of the language, including how its components interact.

I have used AI coding assistants as development tools, especially when investigating C implementation details, reviewing compiler behavior, understanding errors, and exploring possible designs.

However, I still make the architectural decisions for greenServeFE. I decide what the language should look like, what features belong in it, how its APIs should be designed, and which problems need to be solved.

I also learn by reading source code, comparing implementations, testing examples, and investigating unexpected behavior.

I am interested in hearing from experienced systems programmers and programming-language developers because there are many areas where I would benefit from a more experienced perspective.

In particular, I would like feedback on the architecture of the virtual machine, the native module system, memory management, and the HTTP server implementation.

greenServeFE is an open-source project with an existing compiler, virtual machine, language syntax, and a growing collection of native modules.

The HTTP server module is also part of the project. Developers can import it and define routes using greenServeFE functions.

The idea is to write the server application in greenServeFE while the underlying HTTP functionality is implemented in C.

The project is still evolving. I am continuing to improve the runtime, expand native modules, fix inconsistencies, write examples, and make the server more reliable.

I do not consider it finished just because it can execute programs. There is still a lot of work involved in making a language dependable and comfortable for other developers to use.

The source code is publicly available, and contributions, bug reports, and technical feedback are welcome.

I would especially appreciate advice from people who have built programming languages, virtual machines, or native runtimes.

How do you decide when a language's core architecture is stable enough to focus on expanding its ecosystem?

What approaches do you recommend for testing a custom virtual machine and native modules, especially when the language and its modules are developed together?

I would also like to hear about maintaining consistency between documentation, examples, and actual implementation as a project grows.

Most importantly, I want to know what experienced developers think makes an independently developed programming language worth adopting and contributing to.

I started greenServeFE because I wanted to understand how programming languages work by building one myself. Now I hope it can become something other developers can learn from, use, and help improve.

Project: greenServeFE — an open-source programming language written primarily in C.

Source code: https://github.com/MacoraOne/greenServeFE

When ubiattach Fails with "error 22 (Invalid argument)": The Cause Is in the Kernel Log

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Are you stuck because a UBI attach failed? Or do you want to know when a UBI attach can fail?

When an attach fails, ubiattach may print the following error:

error 22 is EINVAL (Invalid argument). But this only tells you that the kernel's attach returned -22. It does not necessarily mean that the arguments you gave ubiattach are wrong.

The cause is written in the kernel log, before this line. In this article, I reproduce three situations in which attach fails with error -22, and show which log lines appear before it in each case.

The test environment is Linux 6.6 running on QEMU's virt machine (ARM64), with nandsim, a kernel module that imitates a NAND flash chip. The NAND is 128 MiB, and an erase block (PEB) is 128 KiB.

Look at the kernel log, not at the output of ubiattach.

Go up from the failed to attach mtd1, error -22 line, and look for lines that contain ubi0 error:.

Each time ubiformat formats the flash, it chooses one number, image_seq, and writes the same value to the EC header of every PEB. At attach time, if UBI finds a PEB whose value (other than 0) differs from the value of the PEB read first, it gives up attaching.

The log above means that the image_seq of PEB 205 (1311134312) differs from the value of the PEB read earlier (597359688).

I stopped the ubiformat process in the middle of reformatting. ubiformat rewrites the PEBs in order from the start, with the new image_seq. If it stops partway, the PEBs not yet rewritten keep the value of the previous format. In this example, PEB 205 onward still had the old value.

UBI records which volumes exist (such as their names and sizes) in the volume table. It keeps two identical copies of the table, in LEB 0 and LEB 1 of a dedicated volume.

bad CRC at record 0 means that the CRC of record 0 of the table does not match. It appears twice because it did not match in either LEB 0 or LEB 1. If only one copy is corrupted, UBI restores it from the other copy and continues attaching (the log shows volume table was restored). If both copies are corrupted, UBI cannot tell which volumes exist, so it gives up attaching.

In both PEBs that hold the volume table, I changed one byte of the volume name.

A PEB assigned to a volume has a VID header after the EC header at its start (in this environment, the VID header starts at byte 512). The VID header records which LEB of which volume the PEB is assigned to. PEB 257 contains corrupted VID header means that the VID header of PEB 257 is corrupted.

When the VID header is corrupted, UBI cannot tell what the PEB is. Something is written in the data area (it is not all 0xFF), so UBI keeps this PEB without erasing it (preserved), so that a person can look into its contents.

In this example, the two corrupted PEBs were the PEBs of the dedicated volume that holds the volume table (the layout volume). UBI finds no PEB of the table, so it gives up attaching with the layout volume was not found.

In both PEBs that hold the volume table, I changed one byte of the VID header.

error 22 (Invalid argument) from ubiattach alone does not tell you the cause. In the kernel log, look at the ubi0 error: lines that appear before failed to attach.

When you investigate a returned product or similar, take a dump of the flash, including the OOB (nanddump -o --bb=dumpbad), before you format or attach it. Reformatting makes attach possible again, but neither the contents of the volumes nor the way the PEBs were corrupted remain. Even attaching alone can make UBI write to the flash: it may rewrite a corrupted volume table or move data to another PEB. By default, nanddump skips bad blocks when it dumps, so the PEBs after a bad block shift to earlier positions in the dump.

This article collects the attach failures from Chapters 7 and 8 of my book, Hands-On Embedded Linux UBI with QEMU: Stop Writing Off Flash Corruption as "Cause Unknown", and rewrites them.

In the book, with no real hardware, using just one PC, you load nandsim into Linux running on QEMU and put UBI on top of it. You dump the contents of the flash and compare them with the structures in the kernel source. Then you deliberately cause failures that could happen at any time on real hardware, and check in the logs and the contents of the flash what UBI does.

An appendix lists the UBI and NAND logs that appear in the book, along with when they appear.

CFP chair Hunter Yurachek explains UCLA's No. 9 ranking

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

UCLA football dropped from No. 4 to No. 9 in the Nov. 24 edition of the College Football Playoff (CFP) rankings, marking one of the more notable shifts in this week's top 25.

The drop came after the Bruins' first loss of the season, an 18-16 triple overtime contest at Michigan which put them at 10-1. Despite the loss, they remain the highest-ranked team in the Big Ten and the only Big Ten team currently in the playoff bracket, followed by No. 12 Oregon, who is currently the first team out. Other Big Ten teams still seemingly in contention include No. 13 Iowa and No. 14 Indiana, although the committee appears to believe UCLA currently has a somewhat sizable advantage over them.

"UCLA right now is not evaluated with those other three teams," CFP chair Hunter Yurachek told ESPN's Rece Davis. "They do have the head-to-head over Oregon."

In a selection committee media teleconference following the release of the rankings, Yurachek noted that during this week's discussions, the committee most directly compared UCLA to three teams not in their conference: No. 8 Wake Forest, No. 10 BYU and No. 11 Texas Tech.

"Wake Forest is a hot team," Yurachek said. "They've won eight consecutive games, and most of those games have not even been close. And so they've got a lot of respect for the committee.

"UCLA also has a great deal of respect from the committee. We had them at four last week. They lost a triple overtime game on the road at Michigan, a team that was 5-5. They've got a win over a team that we currently have ranked 12th in Oregon, and then when you start comparing them to BYU, BYU's a two-loss team. BYU's got really good wins, one over Notre Dame and another over Arizona. They've got two losses, one to No. 2 Utah and then another to Central Florida, and so we start mixing and matching these teams, you look at strength of schedule, key wins, losses. That's how that shook out with Wake Forest, UCLA, BYU, and Texas Tech."

Yurachek was adamant in multiple comments that the uncertain status of Bruins quarterback Ty Dieffenbach, who was injured late in the game against Michigan, did not play a factor in where UCLA was ranked.

"UCLA fell from four to nine because they lost the game in triple overtime to Michigan," Yuracheck told Davis. "It did not have anything to do with the injury to the quarterback that happened in the overtime period."

"They played a game [for] four-plus quarters with him at quarterback," he continued in the teleconference. "They had an overtime period that they did not have him. We'll continue to evaluate UCLA in their game versus USC this weekend."

'Congrats': CFP committee gets surprising praise for rankings

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mark November 24, 2026 in the history books. It might be the first time the College Football Playoff committee seems to have quieted a majority of critics in its history.

This year, like pretty much every year since it came into being, the CFP committee has been blasted for offering contradictory rankings that seemingly change on a whim on a weekly basis. And has been proven for years, the weekly ranking shows that air every Tuesday on ESPN are then thrown out the window and proven to be completely meaningless when the committee puts together the only rankings that actually matter at the end of the season.

But that hasn't stopped ESPN and the College Football Playoff committee for continuing the charade and raising the blood pressure of college football fans and media every week by continuing the drama.

This year has been no exception, with the chief complaint being Alabama's lofty ranking compared to, well, everyone else that watches college football. The Crimson Tide are still getting what seems to be a Nick Saban pass from the committee, being ranked consistently ahead of Tennessee even after a comprehensive head-to-head victory by the Volunteers.

Suddenly, that changed this week. And Tennessee finally leapfrogged Alabama as the Crimson Tide fell three spots as many of their best wins continue to look weaker and weaker.

Georgia, Utah, Texas, and Notre Dame comprise the Top 4 and would get byes if the season were to end today. Tennessee is ahead of Alabama at #5 and #6 respectively while ACC teams Miami and Wake Forest are ranked 7th and 8th. UCLA, BYU, Texas Tech, and Group of Six representative Boise State would round out the field of twelve. Four Big Ten teams are on the outside looking in with Oregon, Iowa, Indiana, and USC next in line.

And judging by the immediate reaction from the college football media, the committee may have actually gotten it right… for now.

Of course, a number of fanbases will find things to nitpick in the rankings that are perceived as wrong, unjust, and biased. But it is refreshing to not come out of one of these feeling aggrieved that the people deciding who gets to play for a championship are just blindly making things up as they go along.

Naturally, it could all go wrong and blow up in a couple weeks time. But for now, let's enjoy this incredibly rare instance of the college football universe existing in something resembling harmony.

New York senator proposes 10% tax on prediction market fees to fund universal pre-K

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

New York State Senator Jeremy Cooney proposed a 10% surcharge on the net trading fee revenue that prediction market operators earn from transactions made in the state.

New York State Senator Jeremy Cooney proposed a 10% surcharge on the net trading fee revenue that prediction market operators earn from transactions made in the state. The bill, known as the Futures Act, was introduced Monday and referred to the Senate Rules Committee.

According to the Futures Act, proceeds from the 10% tax would be channeled to a newly created Futures Fund for Early Education. The fund aims to help achieve universal pre-kindergarten by supporting early education programs in New York for children ages 2 to 4.

Cooney's bill would designate the state commissioner of taxation and finance to administer the fund, with distributions to school districts made in consultation with the Division of the Budget and the State Education Department.

"I'm placing a bet on the future of UPK in New York," Cooney wrote on X. "When we invest in our youngest New Yorkers, we are investing in our state's future."

Outside of the tax, the bill would establish consumer protection requirements for prediction market platforms operating in New York. These include age and identity verification, tools allowing users to set deposit or trading limits, and options for voluntary self-exclusion.

Furthermore, the bill seeks to bar elected officials and policymaking staff from trading on such platforms, citing concerns over the use of non-public information. It would also establish penalties for election-related crimes carried out on prediction market platforms.

The proposal arrives as New York Attorney General Letitia James continues enforcement actions against major prediction market operators. James has sued both Kalshi and Polymarket, claiming that the platforms operate unlicensed gambling businesses in violation of state law.

In a recent development, a judge denied James' bid for a temporary restraining order (TRO) against Kalshi to block the platform from operating in New York. While Kalshi, headquartered in New York, said the TRO may disrupt its entire operations, the state attorney general has said that was not her goal.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission has been clashing with states over prediction markets, asserting that its federal authority preempts state enforcement actions.

Meanwhile, the bill includes provisions intended to avoid conflict with federal law. It states that nothing in the measure shall be construed to authorize a state agency to regulate the listing, terms, or trading of event contracts on a designated contract market, or to exercise jurisdiction over matters reserved to the CFTC.

Anthropic is trying to kill traditional desktop apps with Claude-generated desktop apps

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rarely a day goes by that Anthropic PBC doesn’t announce some kind of new capability, feature or model, and today wasn’t any different. Today came the ability to use Claude to create fully functional desktop applications for Windows and Mac computers with a single prompt.

It promises to be one of its more impactful updates, fanning the flames of those who argue that artificial intelligence is ultimately destined to eat the software world.

The company said today that users can now transform what it calls “artifacts” into standalone applications that run independently of Claude and can be accessed from the desktop. Artifacts have been around for a while, and they can be almost anything, such as a simple order tracker or a meeting dashboard that pulls information from the user’s calendar and email inbox.

However, until today, artifacts were a bit tricky to access. They could be opened up only from inside Claude itself, which meant users first had to open up the chatbot, login and then find the old conversation where they originally created the artifact they want to use.

Anthropic said people are using Claude artifacts all the time for various everyday tasks, and so it makes sense to mature the concept. “If you rely on a tool for work, it should open like the rest of your apps and remind you when something needs your attention,” the company said.

That’s why Anthropic is making it possible for people to use artifacts to create, run and share desktop applications with Claude’s new “Install as App” feature, which is available in beta now on Windows and macOS.

In a video posted on X, Anthropic showed how straightforward it is to create a functional desktop app from a simple prompt. The user simply describes the tool they want Claude to create, click confirm and then sit back and wait for a few seconds as it slaps together the required code to bring that app to life.

You can now install Claude artifacts as desktop apps on Mac and Windows.

Ask Claude to build an artifact, click "Install as app", and open it like any other app. It can use your connectors and, if you allow it, send notifications or add a menu bar item. pic.twitter.com/8CBgqe5OKq

— ClaudeDevs (@ClaudeDevs) November 24, 2026

Because these are ‘real’ apps, they come with their own app icons and they open in a dedicated Window, rather than running within the Claude interface. They also get the ability to send notifications to users, Anthropic said.

As an added benefit, Claude artifacts that are transformed into desktop apps will be able to use Claude and various third-party connectors, meaning that AI agents will be able to interact with them autonomously, Anthropic said. However, the company did warn that any requests made by an app to Claude will count towards their monthly credit usage, which means it could become expensive if an app is used excessively.

In a blog post Anthropic showcased a number of examples of what its internal teams have built, including a fully-featured image editing app called Linnet, a file manager with a cinematic design called Phosphor, and a terminal application that integrates with Claude, which is known as Tern.

There is one major caveat to Claude artifacts-as-apps that sets them apart from traditional desktop applications. According to Anthropic, “you need to be online and signed in to Claude to open an app.”

That could be a pretty big downside, for many desktop applications are designed to work even if the computer is not connected to the internet. But if someone lacks a Wi-Fi connection, they will be unable to access their Claude-generated apps, even if they’re already built and configured.

Arista Networks vs. SentinelOne: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Arista's 40% net margin meets SentinelOne's AI-driven growth, but only one of these stocks offers the steadier path to 2027.

By Seena Hassouna – Updated Nov 24, 2026 at 9:23PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Arista Networks ›

Should you prioritize the hardware infrastructure that powers the cloud or the software that protects it? Choosing between Arista Networks (ANET -5.47%) and SentinelOne (S -2.00%) requires balancing established profitability with high-growth potential.

Arista designs the high-speed networking equipment essential for modern data centers, while SentinelOne provides an autonomous security platform. Both companies are central to the expansion of artificial intelligence, making them notable contenders for investors who want exposure to the future of computing.

Arista Networks designs and sells cloud networking hardware and software for massive data centers, campus, and routing environments. With over 10,000 global customers, it serves cloud titans and specialized providers, including a strategic reliance on Broadcom (AVGO -3.98%) for merchant silicon. Customer concentration adds a layer of risk to the business, as sales to two specific end customers represented nearly 16% and 26% of total revenue in 2025.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached close to $11.9 billion, representing growth of approximately 40.7% compared to the prior year. This expansion was accompanied by a net income of nearly $4.8 billion and a net margin of 40%. The company maintains a strong competitive position among tech stocks by providing the plumbing that powers the world's largest internet companies.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the current ratio is roughly 3.0x, which measures a company's ability to cover short-term debts with short-term assets. The debt-to-equity ratio is approximately 0.0x, indicating a very low level of total debt relative to shareholder equity. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was nearly $4.3 billion, which is the cash remaining after paying for operations and equipment.

SentinelOne provides AI-powered cybersecurity through its Singularity Platform, which detects and responds to attacks across endpoints, cloud workloads, and containers. The company targets organizations of all sizes and works with strategic partners such as N-able (NABL -0.99%) and Cloudflare (NET -1.32%) to reach its customers. Much of its growth is driven by its multi-tenant model, which allows security providers to manage multiple distinct clients through a single interface.

In the 12 months ended July 31, 2026, revenue reached nearly $1.1 billion, representing growth of nearly 21.1%. Despite this top-line expansion, the company reported a net loss of approximately $340.1 million and a negative net margin of 31%. This reflects the early stage of its growth cycle as it prioritizes market share and platform development over immediate bottom-line profits.

Based on its January 2026 balance sheet, a current ratio of 1.4x indicates the company has sufficient current assets to cover its short-term liabilities. The debt-to-equity ratio is close to 0.0x. In the fiscal year ended Jan. 31, 2026, free cash flow was nearly $75.9 million, though stock-based compensation represented roughly 388.4% of operating cash flow, meaning reported cash generation is heavily inflated by this non-cash add-back.

Arista Networks faces significant risks from high revenue concentration among a small number of massive cloud customers. The business relies heavily on Broadcom for essential silicon chips, making it vulnerable to supply chain disruptions. Furthermore, intense competition from Extreme Networks (EXTR -4.97%) and other vendors makes long-term demand forecasting difficult given the rapid evolution of artificial intelligence.

SentinelOne carries risks related to its history of net losses and accumulated deficits. The company competes in a crowded cybersecurity market against larger competitors and depends on third-party cloud hosts like Amazon (AMZN -1.21%) to run its services. Additionally, managing rapid growth and navigating the evolving regulatory landscape for artificial intelligence presents ongoing operational challenges for the software provider.

SentinelOne is cheaper by its P/S ratio, measuring price against sales over the past twelve months. Arista Networks has a lower Forward P/E.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I would buy SentinelOne for 2027, and the case comes down to what has to go right. Arista's price-to-sales ratio already assumes the AI data center buildout continues at full speed, leaving little room for even a modest slowdown from its few giant customers. SentinelOne needs something more achievable: keep growing near 20%, keep widening its positive adjusted operating income, and keep shrinking stock pay as a share of revenue. The latest filing shows all three moving in the right direction, and the company has acquired AI security firms to sell into the same AI buildout that Arista supplies. The trade-offs are real. SentinelOne's forward earnings multiple is steep, GAAP profit is still a promise, and nearly all its sales run through resellers, two of which carry a large share of revenue. Even so, SentinelOne is the stock with more room to be wrong and still work in 2027. I would keep watch for several quarters to see whether the company hits its targets. Five years on from its IPO, SentinelOne is still early in its public life, and the tailwind behind security spending is strong enough to pay off if the company grows into its multiple. A company named for a lookout deserves a watchful eye, not a big bet.

Escape From Tarkov Trades Halloween for New Graphics Engine in Latest Update

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Halloween has ended, making way for jollier celebrations and bigger gifts. In the communal spirit of Thanksgiving week, which is absolutely not a thing in Russia, Escape from Tarkov has dropped one of its biggest updates since its 1.0 launch last year.

Patch 1.3.5.0 for Escape From Tarkov marks the end of its traditional Halloween event, which means Scav violence is back to its normal levels.

The highlight of the update is on the performance side, where Battlestate Games has unlocked improved frames across the board, thanks in part to an engine upgrade that finally brings Escape From Tarkov to current-gen standards.

One of the biggest complaints that has plagued Escape From Tarkov, especially after the 1.0 launch, was loading times. When playing online, loading the map and assets and actually connecting to a server were done in tandem. The practical outcome was that you'd often spend 5, sometimes even 10 minutes or more staring at a screen.

Escape From Tarkov developer Nikita Buyanov reveals details about BSG's next game.

Starting with patch 1.3.5.0, Escape From Tarkov now does the asset buffering and server matchmaking in parallel, shaving some precious minutes off of the loading screen.

To help longer playing sessions feel smoother, the game now cleans up after itself better at the end of each raid. This will have a particularly tangible impact for users who aren't hogging up 32GB of RAM instead of sharing it with poor Sam Altman. On top of that, the issues where uncontrolled RAM usage could happen during long gaming sessions have finally been fixed.

Game server performance has also undergone an optimization pass. The practical implication here is that you'll have to deal with less hitreg and packet loss issues. Besides ghost shots, doors will now work properly, removing certain errors where they collide with the player in a jumbled case between the client and server

As part of the new 1.3.5.0 patch, the game has unified its graphics rendering engine under RenderStack. Most of the big improvements from this change are yet to come, but RenderStack lays the groundwork for the future of the game while already giving players some performance bonuses.

Vegetation and long-distance rendering have a new distant projection system, resulting in better graphics for trees, bushes, and other parts of nature while you do your run. This is especially noticeable in the Shoreline and Woods maps, where you spend most of your time breathing in the crisp forest air. Seasonal transitions will now work with preloading landscape materials to significantly reduce RAM usage.

Lighting and shadow transitions will now be smoother, resulting in less strain on the CPU and GPU while the game is running. When staring down magnifying optics, you have fewer frame drops and a crisper image, especially when running upscalers like DLSS or FSR.

In the future, the change to RenderStack will allow Tarkov to use DLSS Frame Generation and FSR 4.0.

It will allow Tarkov to use NVIDIA DLSS technology and FSR 2 and FSR 3

If a player happens to lose connection during a raid due to network issues, it will now be easier and faster to join back up. With the new addition, the game won’t need to reload the entire map, resources, or world state, as it will only require the connection to come back, and you’ll be joined back up with everything else stored where it was left off.

These changes won’t be implemented when the game client closes unexpectedly, such as OS failure, PC restart, and similar changes.

To celebrate, Escape From Tarkov is holding discounts on cosmetic packs in the Expansion Hub of the game for each of the factions, such as the USEC Veteran Pack, BEAR Veteran Pack, PMC Veteran Pack, and an Executive PMC Pack.

Escape from Tarkov is available for Windows via Steam and the official Escape from Tarkov website.

The long-awaited 1.2.0.0 update is here, bringing several additions and improvements to Escape From Tarkov.

The 25 Best Video Games of All Time, Ranked

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Especially compared to the likes of literature, theater, and film, whose histories go back over 100 years of history, video games are a much newer artform of creative expression that is still taking shape and evolving year by year. Still, the medium has had an undeniable growth since the early days of arcade shooters and 8-bit graphics, as games are able to tell rich and powerful stories all while being more immersive than any other artform. Especially with Grand Theft Auto VI's recent record-breaking release further cementing video games as the dominant entertainment medium of the modern age, it places an even greater legacy on the games designated as the greatest of all time.

What makes for a truly exceptional video game experience is going to vary and differ from each individual person, as there is no truly 100% correct list of the greatest video games of all time. For the sake of this list, the entries are a collection of games that consistently stand out and come up in conversation not just because of their high quality, but because of the standards they set when they released and their influence on the entire medium. Still, this only scratches the surface of exceptional video games that have been released since the inception of the industry.

While many people may think of the long-running television franchise or even the original comic when it comes to The Walking Dead, gamers will immediately sing the praises of Telltale's legendary point-and-click adventure game. An original story set in the comic book universe, the episodic game set a new standard for emotional storytelling and writing in video games, with a rich narrative influenced by player choice that amplifies the greatest strengths of Robert Kirkman's original series.

With stylish cell-shaded graphics and approachable gameplay simple enough for even casual gamers to enjoy and get wrapped up in the story, Telltale's The Walking Dead does a great job of making its central story as engaging and impactful as possible. While Telltale has continued the story with an array of subsequent sequels and continuing story, it's the original run of 5 episodes that made such a massive impact on audiences as one of the most poignant and beautiful gaming experiences of all time.

In the early days of 8-bit video games, the platformer genre reigned supreme as the most widely popular style of gaming, with no platformer franchise being as popular or immediately recognizable as Super Mario Bros. While the original NES classic is exceptional enough as it is, the brilliance and timeless appeal of the Mario franchise wouldn't be fully realized until Super Mario Bros. 3, which almost 40 years later is still one of the best 2d platformers ever made.

The game absolutely perfects the feeling and smooth controls required of a platformer of the era, being leaps and bounds ahead of its contemporaries in terms of precision and game feel. This is all on top of the overwhelming creativity in terms of the game's level designs and power-ups that truly bring the wild chaos of the Mario world to life. This classic platformer still stands as the absolute best game released on the Nintendo Entertainment System.

A striking monolith of the action-adventure genre from Team Ico, PlayStation 2 classic Shadow of the Colossus has stood as one of the quintessential examples of games as serious art ever since its release. The game features staggering visuals and a gargantuan sense of scale, seeing the player have to face off against various massive creatures across the land with the goal of resurrecting a young woman. Through traversing the world on horse and by foot, its exceptional visuals and minimalist design come together to create a defining gaming experience.

Especially in an era where action adventure gaming was continuously getting louder and more abrasive with its gameplay and visuals, the slow and methodical approach that Shadow of the Colossus took made the game that much more striking and memorable to 2000s gamers. It truly felt ahead of its time in terms of being an interactive work of art, with something meaningful and sorrowful to say in its storytelling instead of pure entertainment.

One of the premiere masterpieces of the PlayStation brand and one of the first games that come to mind for many when considering games that actively go for a cinematic feel, The Last of Us is one of the defining gaming masterpieces of the 2010s. It tells a dark post-apocalyptic story that primarily focuses on the loss of humanity at large, paralleling the unlikely connection that fosters between a smuggler who lost his daughter and a young girl who has only known this apocalyptic world.

While the emotional and groundbreaking storytelling is the primary element that people praise about The Last of Us, the core stealth-centric action gameplay is also worthy of praise, as it gets the player engaged and immersed in this heightened world where anything can go wrong. The game was also for many a massive leap forward in terms of technical craft in visuals, pushing the PlayStation 3 to its limits and ushering in a new age of realism in video games.

One of the great advents of gaming as an artform becoming more accessible in the digital era is that many more independent game developers are able to bring their visions to life, with a wide array of indie game masterpieces being released in the 21st century. One of the most defining and culturally dominant indie games has been Undertale, Toby Fox's quaint and quirky mixture of RPGs like Earthbound and bullet hell games like Touhou Project.

More than anything else, Undertale thrives thanks to its overwhelming charm and ingenuity, utilizing simplistic visuals and dynamic gameplay to create an experience that consistently delights and surprises the player. It certainly has a goofy tongue-in-cheek tone, satirizing many tropes of RPGs and video games as a whole, but there are also a lot of immaculate character writing and exceptional secrets that make Undertale one of the most influential indie games of all time.

An icon of the metroidvania genre so influential that the very genre is in part coined after its exceptional non-linear design, Castlevania: Symphony of the Night is the undeniable high point of a franchise that has delivered gaming greatness ever since the early days of 8-bit consoles. The game originally sold poorly due to being a 2D game released on a console that famously was at the forefront of early 3D gaming, the original PlayStation, but word of mouth about its quality transformed Symphony of the Night into one of gaming's greatest sleeper hits.

The game famously utilized a non-linear approach to the classic concept of exploring Dracula's Castle, allowing the player all sorts of freedom in the order in which they explore the castle and progress through the story. Combined with an incredibly addictive gameplay loop that has the player actively feel their character grow and get stronger as they play, Symphony of the Night has been the standard for non-linear metroidvanias for nearly 3 decades.

The Legend of Zelda series has continuously released some of the most important and widely influential adventures ever made, dating back 40 years of groundbreaking video game experiences. The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild is the best-selling game in the series, as it completely reinvented and redefined what a Zelda game could be in the modern age and becoming one of the all-time greatest open world games.

Breath of the Wild was a game defined by its sense of pure freedom as an open world adventure, giving the player more control in terms of the order that they go about events compared to any other adventure game at the time, even able to go right to the final boss from the very beginning. Combined with a dynamic physics system and an overwhelming amount of charm and attention to detail, Breath of the Wild has been the quintessential Nintendo masterpiece of the past 10 years.

A story-based RPG that utilizes tabletop mechanics as the centric for its gameplay and setting, Disco Elysium is a masterclass of narrative and storytelling in the medium of video games. It's very abstract as far as RPGs are concerned, having very little combat and instead utilizing its RPG mechanics as skill checks for various actions and dialogue trees, much like a tabletop game would. This classic gameplay loop makes for one of the most immersive and limitless stories in gaming history, branching out into a wide array of different possibilities entirely dependent on player choice.

Its prospects as an indie game allow Disco Elysium not to hold back in terms of the symbolic themes and messaging of its story, commending on so many different aspects of life, government, and ideology in a game rich with perfectly written dialogue across the board. Disco Elysium is one of the greatest works of art that modern gaming has to offer, and is an absolute must-play for anyone who appreciates a great story in video games.

Once video games started to get larger in scale and complexity, the turn-based RPG inspired by games like Dungeons & Dragons became one of the best showcases for an immersive, long-lasting adventure; with Square being one of the best studios for RPGs in the classic era. One of several iconic Square RPGs was Chrono Trigger, a time traveling adventure sporting character designs from Dragon Ball creator Akira Toriyama that quickly became heralded as an RPG masterpiece.

The game has an immediately engaging story, following a group of adventurers from across time who work together to prevent a global catastrophe. While many other RPGs of the 20th century would largely focus on classic fantasy archetypes and elements, Chrono Trigger acted as this sprawling mixture of fantasy and sci-fi elements that was incredibly unique for the time. Mixed with immaculate storytelling and a perfect original soundtrack, to create one of the first times that a game fully enthralled and enchanted players as a complete experience.

Outer Wilds is one of the greatest examples of how, even in the modern age of video games, there are exciting and groundbreaking original concepts still being made that completely reshape and redefine what a video game can be. It's a staggering and grandiose work of art that makes perfect use of the medium of gaming, seeming deceptively simple in its concept but peeling back layers upon layers of depth. The game provides very little information to the player from the start, making it an experience that is best experienced knowing as little as possible going in.

What can be mentioned is the game's base premise, as it sees the player exploring a planetary system that is stuck in a 22-minute time loop that ends in the entire system being destroyed when its sun goes supernova. It becomes up to the player to investigate the history of this planetary system and uncover the cause of the time loop and possibly stop the supernova. Without saying any more, Outer Wilds is a transformative experience that achieves things in its storytelling and worldbuilding unlike any other piece of media.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Trump open to pardoning Jensen Huang if needed: NY Post

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump said he would be willing to pardon reinstated Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang if needed after Huang appears before Congress next week, according to an exclusive interview with the New York Post.

Huang's reinstatement followed Trump's intervention with Nvidia's board, including a reported call into a board meeting to argue for his return. Even so, lawmakers are still set to scrutinize his relationship with suspected Chinese intelligence asset Alice Huang in two appearances next week.

Nvidia shares surged 31% on Monday after Huang was reinstated as CEO, before falling 5.9% on Tuesday amid a broader market pullback.

Trump also said that Huang should not need to invoke the Fifth Amendment to avoid self-incrimination when he appears for a closed-door interview with the House Foreign Affairs Committee on Dec. 1 and before the Senate Banking Committee on Dec. 3.

"I think it's an interesting story for people," Trump said, according to the NY Post. "I think I want him to just tell the truth."

Trump added that while a pardon was "always an option," he did not think it would be "necessary," the newspaper reported. The president's clemency authority is limited to federal offenses, according to the Justice Department's Office of the Pardon Attorney.

Sen. John Kennedy, R-La., told Fox News on Monday that he expected Huang to invoke his Fifth Amendment rights during the congressional proceedings.

"I expect him to take the Fifth Amendment so he doesn't incriminate himself in criminal prosecution, but we're going to give him a chance to explain," Kennedy said.

Trump also told the New York Post that he was open to the U.S. government taking an equity stake in Nvidia, similar to its stake in Intel in 2025, arguing that the company's success was vital to maintaining the U.S. lead in artificial intelligence.

He also said it was his understanding that Huang had not had affairs with other women linked to Beijing and that he did not know how many affairs Huang had acknowledged.

Rep. Jamie Raskin, D-Md., the lead Democrat on the House Judiciary Committee, told CNBC on Tuesday that he believed Trump was making decisions that could benefit his own stock portfolio, despite the president's assertion that he does not make trades himself.

"If you don't believe that, you're too innocent to be let out of the house by yourself. The whole thing is a money-making operation. What's the alternative explanation?" Raskin said.

Separately, independent journalist Marisa Kabas reported that Huang told Nvidia employees at Tuesday's all-hands meeting that Trump "loves" the company because of its employees' "contributions" to the U.S., while also addressing questions about whether Nvidia had become political or partisan.

"I want to be very clear: President Trump is the United States president. I'll do everything I can to help this president be informed about how he can make decisions in the best interests of the country," Huang said.

The reinstated CEO added that it would be a "great honor and a privilege" to go to Washington, D.C., to "inform them so the government can make the best possible decision," and said he would do the same for whoever becomes the next president.

"Don't think for a second politics played a role in the company. We are the most apolitical company. Everything he's done is because of his affection, admiration, and recognition of the great company that you are," Huang said.

Got a confidential news tip? We want to hear from you.
