GTA 6 cloud streaming comes to PS5 and PlayStation Portal

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You no longer need to free up 212GB on your PS5 for Rockstar’s blockbuster.

The PlayStation Store page for the game now states that "PS5 and PS Portal cloud streaming [is] supported only with Premium subscription." I bought GTA 6 on a separate account from the one that my PS Plus subscription is on, so I haven't been able to test it yet, unfortunately.

A report last month suggested Xbox would have exclusive cloud streaming rights for GTA 6. However, Xbox Chief Strategy Officer Matthew Ball clarified that wasn't the case.

Much like with Xbox Cloud Gaming, it doesn't seem that there's a way for PlayStation users to stream their copy of GTA 6 to PC via the cloud. Remote play from your console to PC still works (I forgot about that option and it's about to destroy my productivity).

The maker of Asobi, a third-party app that supported PS Plus Premium streaming, said last week Sony was blocking its connections to stream PS5 games from the service. Cloud versions of PS3 and PS4 titles and remote play from a PS5 console still work on Asobi. Sony is also shutting down its own PlayStation Plus app for PC next month because barely anyone was using it, but that's mainly for PS3 and PS4 games anyway.

It's perhaps worth noting that PS Plus Premium will stream GTA 6 at PS5 quality. The Xbox Cloud Gaming streams are limited to Xbox Series S graphics settings. Xbox Cloud Gaming also has time limits as of this month, while PS Plus Premium does not.

Sony announced on Monday that GTA 6 was the biggest game release in the platform's history. More than 39 million unique players explored Leonida on PS5 on the game's first weekend, logging 683 million hours of total gameplay. Developer Rockstar Games said it sold 32.5 million pre-orders of GTA 6, generating $3.79 billion in day-one retail sales.

A PC version of GTA 6 is all but certainly on the way, perhaps as soon as early next year. Efforts are underway to recompile the leaked Xbox build for PC as well.

I Finally Bought Nvidia Stock During the Sell-Off. Here's the Price I'd Pay to Buy More.

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I finally invested in Nvidia after its sell-off. Here's why I bought at $155 and the price that would tempt me to add.

By Matt DiLallo – Nov 24, 2026 at 1:15PM EST

I've wanted to own shares of Nvidia (NVDA -7.46%) for a while. However, while the company's artificial intelligence (AI) business continued delivering extraordinary growth, its premium valuation kept me on the sidelines.

The recent sell-off finally changed that equation. I capitalized on the recent downdraft in Nvidia stock, buying five shares for $155 apiece, investing $775 in the chipmaker. Even though I'm excited about Nvidia's long-term potential, I'm not ready to increase my position just yet. Here's why I finally bought Nvidia stock and what I'd need to see before buying more.

Nvidia is growing remarkably fast. The company's revenue grew a blistering 103% in the third quarter to nearly $116 billion, driven by a 110% surge in data center revenue to $108 billion. Its earnings also more than doubled to over $65 billion. The semiconductor company expects its revenue to rise to around $130 billion in the fourth quarter, a roughly 12% sequential increase. Meanwhile, analysts expect Nvidia to earn about $9.67 per share this year, more than triple last year's earnings.

Despite its exponential growth, shares of Nvidia have tumbled more than 30% from their recent peak due to concerns about an AI-spending slowdown and Nvidia's leadership. With its business booming but its share price under pressure, Nvidia's valuation has come down considerably. It currently trades at less than 17x earnings, down from over 45x earlier this year. That much less demanding valuation led me to finally buy a few shares of Nvidia.

Before I bought shares, I ran bear, base, and bull scenarios for what Nvidia could be worth in five years, based on the following assumptions about earnings growth and valuation multiples:

My base case is reasonably conservative. It assumes Nvidia grows its earnings by 75% over the next five years, with a slight improvement in its valuation multiple. That would deliver a double-digit annualized return from my purchase price. That's my sweet spot; I want companies that can deliver double-digit returns from very reasonable assumptions.

Meanwhile, the bull case would produce an even stronger return if it delivers faster growth and multiple expansion. On the other hand, the bear case shows the potential risk. I think the risk/reward has shifted to the point where buying Nvidia looked too compelling to ignore.

While I thought Nvidia was compelling at $155 a share, I didn't buy a full position, given all that's going on with the company. At the time of my purchase, there was significant uncertainty due to the departure of CEO Jensen Huang, who has since returned to lead the company. Additionally, there remain concerns about a potential slowdown in AI spending and Nvidia's role in funding the AI infrastructure build-out.

That's why I want an even better price before adding. My next potential buying zone is in the $140–$145 range. Still, I'd only add if the problems driving the decline were temporary rather than evidence of accounting irregularities or a deteriorating AI investment cycle. I want to be very disciplined because my portfolio already has significant exposure to the AI megatrend. So, adding more of even a high-quality company like Nvidia can still be a risky investment if it represents excessive exposure to the same economic theme.

Nvidia's remarkable growth and more attractive valuation finally convinced me to buy shares. At my $155 purchase price, my base-case projections suggest that this investment could nearly double in value over the next five years.

However, I'm not rushing to buy more. Nvidia still faces important questions about AI infrastructure spending, profitability, and regulatory investigations. I'd be interested in adding to my position around $140 to $145, provided the investment thesis remains intact.

For now, I'm comfortable owning a small piece of this AI powerhouse while waiting for a better opportunity to increase my investment.

David Fincher is being dragged for suspected use of AI in his latest film

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Early screenings of David Fincher’s latest film are raising questions about whether the director used generative AI.

Early screenings of David Fincher’s latest film are raising questions about whether the director used generative AI.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Though David Fincher’s fans had been eagerly anticipating The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, early screenings of the Once Upon a Time in Hollywood sequel starring Brad Pitt have raised questions about whether generative AI was used in the film. People have claimed that the movie is chock-full of uncanny, inconsistent visuals that make it feel more like slop than the sort of polished film you’d expect from the director of sleek productions like Fight Club and The Social Network.

Following a number of Cliff Booth critics screenings that took place last week ahead of the film’s premiere on November 25th, moviegoers rushed to social media to lament what they saw. One of the most vocal reactions came from YouTuber / director Patrick Tomasso, who said on X that he walked out of his screening feeling “completely gutted” and that the film featured “some of the most blatant and egregious AI use” he has ever seen from a major studio. Tomasso also insisted you can see AI-generated footage in Cliff Booth’s trailers that have been posted online, and he was confident that Fincher’s latest film is filled with “slop as you know and loathe it. The shit you can pump out of Higgsfield.”

Others echoed Tomasso’s sentiments, writing that “the amount of AI in the CLIFF BOOTH movie is astounding to an absurd degree” and vowing to “never watch another Fincher, Pitt or Tarintino [sic] movie again.” Yet some critics enjoyed the film and suggested that the bad AI people thought they were seeing might have actually just been bad CGI. Netflix has neither confirmed nor denied whether AI was used in any capacity to make the film, but Netflix’s heavy involvement in the film’s production and the company’s recent investments in generative AI technology left many suspicious.

It did not help that David Fincher, in a recent interview with the Los Angeles Times, responded to questions about his thoughts on AI with “I’m not a Luddite, so I don’t fear the unknown.” Fincher also reasoned that the anti-AI crowd is advocating for artists to stop iterating on their creative processes: “Is everyone satisfied that we have figured this art form out enough that what we really need to do is go back to celluloid? How long have people painted? 40,000 years? We’re talking about a thing that’s a hundred years old and everyone’s going, ‘Stop. Pencils down. No more iterating. It’s not necessary to have at your fingertips the ability to change the color of that person’s eyes.’ Well, it exists. Should we not use it?” The spiel made it sound like he might be jumping on the AI bandwagon.

Set about a decade after Once Upon a Time in Hollywood, Cliff Booth picks up in 1977 at a time when Booth (Pitt) has left his life as a stuntman behind to become a fixer who quietly deals with scandalous problems for Hollywood elites. Cliff still works a number of side gigs as a projectionist and bartender. But the movie is primarily focused on his budding romance with critic-turned-talent agent Karen Crosby (Elizabeth Debicki) and his working relationship with Charlie Peters (Scott Caan), a TV actor who needs Booth’s services to keep his wife (Carla Gugino) from being murdered. At least one trailer for the film features some baffling shots of what looks like people sitting backward in a horse-drawn carriage that make it seem like there might be some generative AI in the mix. It’s hard to tell from the trailers whether that’s actually the case, but in a moment when many creators have been trying to pass off AI generations as human-made art, it’s not surprising that people are suspicious.

Netflix has been incorporating AI into its movies and series for some time now, and it seemed like the company was doubling down earlier this year when it acquired Ben Affleck’s startup, InterPositive, for around $600 million. Rather than turning text into visual outputs like many commercially available AI tools, InterPositive’s AI models are designed to generate assets based on footage captured during a film’s production. According to Netflix, InterPositive’s AI was developed with the intention of making it easier to tweak details like lighting or to remove unwanted background elements. The company also made a big deal of emphasizing that InterPositive’s models were highly customizable and well suited to replicating a human director’s signature style.

Netflix buying InterPositive and absorbing the company’s development team is part of why there has been so much speculation about AI being used in Cliff Booth. If Cliff Booth actually uses AI and the visuals really are as bad as some have said, it would be a major knock against both Netflix and Fincher — a director known for his meticulous (obsessive, some might argue) attention to detail.

It's time to look at Uranus: How to see the solar system's strangest planet at its best this week

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On Wednesday (Nov. 25), the distant ice giant Uranus will be at its best and brightest. Here's when, where and how to look for the seventh planet.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

One of the hardest planets to see in the solar system — the distant ice giant Uranus — reaches its annual opposition on Wednesday (Nov. 25), just a day after the Beaver Supermoon. It's the best time to see the seventh planet from the sun, but you'll need to use stargazing binoculars or a good backyard telescope to find it.

Uranus is faint, slow-moving and rarely a target for casual stargazers. However, on Nov. 25, and, for a few weeks on either side, it will be at its closest point to Earth this year and will appear at its brightest, making it possible to spot.

Opposition occurs when Earth passes between Uranus and the sun; Uranus will be "opposite" the sun, hence, "opposition." The moment of opposition will happen at 6 p.m. ET on Nov. 25, according to EarthSky. Consequently, Uranus will rise in the east around sunset, reach its highest point in the sky around midnight and set in the west around sunrise.

You can find it in the constellation Taurus, close to the Pleiades open star cluster, also known as the Seven Sisters and M45. Uranus will be a few degrees southeast of the cluster, appearing as a faint point of light, about two-fifths of the distance between the Pleiades and Aldebaran, the brightest star in Taurus.

At magnitude +5.6, Uranus is technically visible to the naked eye under very dark skies. However, binoculars or a telescope will make it much easier to find.

Uranus as seen by NASA's Hubble Space Telescope.

At opposition, Uranus will be 18.4 astronomical units (AU) from Earth — about 1.7 billion miles (2.7 billion kilometers) away. Through a telescope, it will appear as a tiny blue-green disk. Its color comes from methane in its atmosphere, which absorbs red wavelengths of sunlight.

Named after Ouranos, the ancient Greek god of the sky and the heavens, according to the Planetary Society , Uranus is one of the least understood planets in the solar system. A day on Uranus lasts for about 17 hours, and the planet rotates sideways, according to NASA. It takes 84 Earth years to orbit the sun.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

Uranus also has 13 faint rings and at least 29 known moons, the most recent of which was found in 2025 by the James Webb Space Telescope. It was named Uranus XXVIII and orbits between the moons Ophelia and Bianca. Many of Uranus' moons are named after characters from the works of William Shakespeare and Alexander Pope.

The distant planet has been visited only once, by NASA's Voyager 2 spacecraft on Jan. 24, 1986. However, contrary to what the probe found, scientists now think the planet emits its own internal heat. That could increase pressure on NASA to prepare a Uranus Orbiter and Probe (UOP), a mission concept the National Academy of Sciences recommended in 2022 as a high-priority planetary science mission for the next decade.

Uranus will next come to opposition on Nov. 30, 2027.

Jamie Carter is a Cardiff, U.K.-based freelance science journalist and a regular contributor to Live Science. He is the author of A Stargazing Program For Beginners and co-author of The Eclipse Effect, and leads international stargazing and eclipse-chasing tours. His work appears regularly in Space.com, Forbes, New Scientist, BBC Sky at Night, Sky & Telescope, and other major science and astronomy publications. He is also the editor of WhenIsTheNextEclipse.com.

Why Is Crypto Down Today? Total Market Cap Crashes -2.5%

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Why Is Crypto Down Today? Cryptocurrency markets woke up red on Tuesday, November 24, 2026. Bitcoin slipped below the psychologically important $60,000 level, trading near $59,800–$60,500 after briefly touching levels above $64,000 the day before.

Ethereum, Solana, and most major altcoins followed lower, with the total crypto market capitalization contracting roughly 1–2% over 24 hours amid elevated liquidations and persistent selling pressure. The question on every trader’s mind is straightforward: why is crypto down today?

🚨 IS THE BOTTOM IN?Bitcoin has been through some brutal crashes before.> 84% in 2018.> 77% in 2022.> And a 56% drop from its $126K peak in 2026.So, what happened this time?The market was already loaded with leverage. When Bitcoin started falling, liquidations kicked… pic.twitter.com/ZGjuafXiyd

Crypto entered the week already wounded. Bitcoin had plunged to a multi-year low near $55,000 over the prior weekend, wiping out leveraged positions and leaving the market in extreme fear territory.

Monday brought a sharp relief rally. Nvidia’s decision to reinstate Jensen Huang as CEO sent the chipmaker’s shares soaring more than 30% and lifted the Nasdaq by around 7%. Risk assets broadly benefited, and Bitcoin briefly joined the party, climbing toward $64,000.

That bounce proved short-lived. By Tuesday morning, the gains had been fully erased. Bitcoin lagged the equity rebound significantly; Nvidia recovered most of its recent losses, while crypto reclaimed only a fraction of its slide. The divergence highlighted that crypto’s problems run deeper than a single day’s equity news.

Several concrete developments converged to pressure prices. First, the Nvidia-driven rally simply faded. Stock futures turned lower on Tuesday as the initial euphoria cooled.

Nasdaq-100 futures dropped around 1%, and related AI and crypto-adjacent equities gave back part of Monday’s gains. Crypto, which had been riding the coattails of the tech surge, sold off in sympathy but more sharply.

Second, institutional flows remained deeply negative. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded approximately $570M in net outflows on Monday, the fifth consecutive session of withdrawals.

BlackRock’s IBIT alone accounted for more than $500M of that figure. November’s cumulative outflows reached roughly $4.71Bn, setting a monthly record and removing a major source of structural demand that had previously supported prices.

Third, derivatives markets amplified the move. More than $800M in crypto futures positions were liquidated over the 24-hour period, more than triple the prior day’s total. Forced selling from both long and short positions created a self-reinforcing cascade as prices fell.

Broader macro factors also weighed on sentiment. Markets continue to price in a potential Federal Reserve rate hike in December, with key inflation data (PCE) due shortly.

Higher rates typically pressure non-yielding assets such as Bitcoin. Additional reports of possible new U.S. tariff measures added another layer of risk-off caution. Lingering effects from the large Binance-related hack earlier in the period further eroded confidence in the sector.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Crypto’s decline today is not the result of a single dramatic headline. Instead, it reflects a short-term relief rally that failed to overcome persistent structural headwinds.

The Nvidia news provided temporary momentum, but without positive ETF flows or a meaningful shift in monetary expectations, that momentum evaporated quickly. Leveraged traders were caught offside, and the resulting liquidations accelerated the drop.

Sentiment remains extremely cautious. The Fear & Greed Index sits deep in extreme fear, at around 10/100, and Bitcoin has still only recovered a modest portion of its November losses.

While some long-term holders and selective buyers may view current levels as attractive, the immediate market is dominated by risk reduction and profit-taking after the failed bounce.

Crypto is down today primarily because Monday’s Nvidia-fueled rally proved unsustainable. Heavy, record-setting Bitcoin ETF outflows continued to drain demand, while a surge in futures liquidations forced additional selling.

Macro uncertainty around interest rates and risk assets provided no supporting backdrop. In short, the market tested a bounce, found insufficient real buying interest, and retreated—leaving Bitcoin back below $60,000 and the broader crypto complex lower.

Onorato dallo stadio Flaminio alla contestazione: "Lotito ha commesso dei gravi errori di comunicazione. Ho il massimo del rispetto di chi oggi protesta"

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

L'Assessore ai Grandi Eventi, Sport, Turismo e Moda, Alessandro Onorato, è intervenuto ai microfoni di Radio Laziale, in cui ha parlato degli ultimi aggiornamenti sullo stadio Flaminio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Stadio Flaminio? In molti pensavano che la proposta della Lazio non fosse reale, invece il progetto esiste. Si è conclusa la Conferenza dei Servizi, che ha espresso parere favorevole, pur prevedendo una serie di prescrizioni e osservazioni. Tra agosto e settembre la Lazio ha presentato alcune proposte di modifica alla Soprintendenza statale, della quale attendiamo le risposte. Qualora venisse confermata l’accettazione delle modifiche, che prevedono una riduzione di pochi metri dell’altezza, il club dovrà rielaborare il piano economico-finanziario. A quel punto il Comune potrà esprimersi sul riconoscimento o meno dell’interesse pubblico. Si ipotizza una riduzione della capienza di 4-5mila posti, arrivando a un totale di circa 45mila spettatori. Sulla sostenibilità economica potremo esprimere un giudizio definitivo soltanto quando riceveremo il piano aggiornato sulla base delle varianti. Un progetto approvato dal Comune deve necessariamente garantire un equilibrio finanziario, mentre l’individuazione di chi finanzierà l’opera rappresenta un passaggio successivo. Attualmente è la Lazio ad attendere la risposta della Soprintendenza statale. Una volta ricevuta, sarà il Comune ad aspettare che la società aggiorni il piano economico-finanziario. Con il sindaco Gualtieri ci auguriamo di concludere questo percorso quanto prima, perché, al di là di Lotito e delle simpatie personali, l’obiettivo è evitare che il Flaminio rimanga in queste imbarazzanti condizioni di degrado. Abbiamo lavorato a lungo su questo impianto, anche perché in passato erano stati presentati altri progetti. In questo caso si tratterebbe di un investimento interamente a carico della Lazio.

Il rapporto con Lotito? Riesco a distinguere il mio ruolo di assessore da quello di tifoso. Come assessore mantengo un atteggiamento imparziale: non contano le simpatie personali, ma i fatti. Il progetto del Flaminio è estremamente complesso, in tanti ci hanno provato senza mai riuscirci. Comprendo anche le preoccupazioni dei residenti, ma già oggi quella zona è difficilmente vivibile. Le normative attuali per autorizzare la riqualificazione del Flaminio sono profondamente diverse rispetto al passato: bisogna dimostrare scientificamente la gestione dei flussi di ingresso e uscita degli spettatori, la sicurezza dell’intera area e molto altro. Per questo sono convinto che, qualora si arrivasse alla conclusione del percorso, ci sarebbe un miglioramento oggettivo per il quartiere rispetto alla situazione attuale durante le partite all’Olimpico.

L’intervista al Foglio su Lotito? Tutto nasce dalla richiesta di un giornalista di commentare le minacce di morte rivolte a Lotito, alle quali ho risposto sottolineando la gravità della vicenda. Il giorno successivo è uscito il titolo “Onorato difende Lotito”, quando nel resto dell’intervista avevo affrontato molte altre questioni. Da tifoso ritengo evidente che Lotito abbia commesso gravi errori nella comunicazione e nel coinvolgimento della tifoseria, e l’ho ribadito più volte. Se poi si vuole far passare il messaggio che io sia contrario alla protesta soltanto perché sono andato una volta allo stadio per evitare che i miei figli di 6 e 10 anni perdessero l’amore per i colori biancazzurri, credo sia un tentativo becero di alimentare ulteriormente le divisioni. Un’altra cosa che mi infastidisce molto è la volontà di distinguere i tifosi della Lazio in categorie di Serie A, B e C. È assurdo: la tifoseria è una sola, non esistono tifosi più puri di altri.

Posso garantire che ogni volta che incontro Lotito gli ribadisco la necessità di assumere atteggiamenti di maggiore distensione e apertura nei confronti della tifoseria. Diffidate dei politici che sfruttano le proteste per ottenere qualche voto in più, perché trovo molto strano questo comportamento. Per me l’amore verso una squadra è qualcosa di sacro, perché senza tifosi il calcio non esiste. Allo stesso tempo, considero surreale che qualcuno possa decidere per gli altri quali comportamenti adottare. Ho visto circolare alcuni video nei quali sembrerebbe che io abbia sostenuto la scelta di Lotito di togliere il merchandising agli Irriducibili. In realtà il mio ragionamento era diverso: vogliamo che le società italiane raggiungano il livello di quelle inglesi, spagnole, tedesche e francesi? Allora servono stadi di proprietà e il merchandising rappresenta una delle principali fonti di ricavo. Riuscite a immaginare un emiro che acquista la Lazio e al quale viene detto che il merchandising è gestito dai tifosi? È un ragionamento poco realistico. Lo dice una persona che da ragazzo frequentava la curva e che ha acquistato liberamente le sciarpe degli Irriducibili e degli altri gruppi organizzati. Ma quello è un altro discorso. Dobbiamo alzare il livello del confronto.

L’altro ieri abbiamo assistito ancora una volta a scontri tra i tifosi del Torino e della Roma. Per quanto tempo dovremo continuare a vedere episodi di violenza legati al calcio? Chi può essere interessato a investire in questo settore se non riusciamo a renderlo sicuro, permettendo ai tifosi di vivere naturalmente la propria passione? Io andavo allo stadio con il club Re Cecconi di Ostia, partendo da piazza Quarto dei Mille con il pullman organizzato e il panino con la frittata. Sono profondamente legato al calcio romantico, ma se in una piazza come Roma vogliamo tornare a sognare, anche noi tifosi dobbiamo avere un approccio diverso. Perché tanti attori e cantanti laziali non dichiarano apertamente la propria fede calcistica? Quanti politici romani lo fanno? Io, Alessandro Cochi e pochi altri. Perché accade questo? Perché tutto diventa complicato, tutto si trasforma in polemica. Dobbiamo superare queste divisioni: siamo un’unica tifoseria, un unico popolo. Nutro il massimo rispetto per chi oggi protesta, perché rappresenta un grande sacrificio e lo comprendo.

Una data per il Flaminio? Se fossi un radioascoltatore e sentissi un assessore indicare una data, impazzirei. Quello che posso garantire, assumendomi la responsabilità del mio ruolo, è che il progetto è concreto e che siamo agli ultimi passaggi prima dell’eventuale approvazione da parte del Comune. Successivamente l’iter prevede un avviso pubblico, con un ulteriore anno di procedure. Se poi Lotito deciderà effettivamente di realizzarlo oppure no, non posso saperlo: bisogna prima arrivare a quel momento. Ma, una volta ottenute tutte le autorizzazioni, possiamo riconoscere che avere lo stadio Flaminio nel cuore della città rappresenterebbe una grande opportunità di crescita per la Lazio? Credo proprio di sì. Oggi incontro tifosi che mi abbracciano e mi chiedono di non far realizzare lo stadio, mentre fino a due anni fa mi chiedevano esattamente il contrario. Il mio invito è semplicemente quello di mantenere la calma.

Comprendo la rabbia, ma ricoprendo il ruolo di assessore del Comune di Roma non ho la libertà di esprimermi come un semplice tifoso. Quando si rappresenta un’istituzione bisogna rispettare i propri doveri istituzionali. Lo stadio della Roma rappresenta una grande opportunità per la città, e lo affermo nonostante sia facilmente immaginabile quale sarà il mio stato d’animo quando andrò al derby. È un progetto importante perché consentirà di riqualificare un’area, porterà un investimento significativo e creerà numerosi posti di lavoro. Quando alcuni tifosi della Lazio mi criticano per le vicende legate a Lotito, ne soffro. Non certamente per una questione elettorale: se avessi voluto ottenere consensi in maniera opportunistica, non avrei mai dichiarato nulla, considerando che a Roma ci sono più romanisti. Ma non è questo che mi interessa. Sono diventato laziale in prima elementare proprio perché nessuno tifava Lazio e sono abituato alle critiche. Essere laziali significa anche saper essere soli, è uno stile di vita. Ciò che mi dispiace maggiormente è vedere un laziale attaccare un altro laziale. È qualcosa che mi fa soffrire. Non riscontro lo stesso atteggiamento nella tifoseria romanista, che a volte arriva persino a negare l’evidenza pur di mantenere il senso di comunità. Stiamo attraversando un momento drammatico nei rapporti tra tifoseria e presidenza, ne sono consapevole. Ma cerchiamo di mantenere la calma.

Indian government has 'lost the plot', says 'cockroach' leader barred from UK flight

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: India's government is 'scared', cockroach movement leader says after travel ban

One of the leaders of India's "cockroach" movement has said the Delhi government has "completely lost the plot" after he was stopped from leaving the country for the UK.

Saurav Das said he was meant to board a flight on Tuesday morning but was told by immigration officers his passport had been "flagged" because of discrepancies involving his date of birth.

In an interview with the BBC, Das said there were no discrepancies in his documents, accusing the Indian government of using cheap tactics to stop activists from travelling and speaking on issues that matter to young people.

The BBC has reached out to the federal home ministry, Delhi police and airport officials for comment.

Saurav told the BBC World Service's Newshour programme the incident showed the government was "scared".

"They are scared of the cockroach movement. They're scared of young people asking them questions because they have never had to deal with a generation that wants to ask questions so head on. So that jitters them."

By not allowing him to travel, he said the government had lowered India's stature in the world.

"It's international news that I am unable to travel to the UK," he said. "And this shows Prime Minister Modi in such poor light.

"It's a way to harass, to target activists, young voices in India by the Modi government."

Das said he had been invited by the Oxford Union, as well as universities including Cambridge and Edinburgh, to speak about the movement.

Ratna Singh, another leader of the Cockroach Janta Party (CJP), wrote: "The manner in which Saurav was not allowed to board today raises deeply troubling questions about the state of freedom of expression and the space being allowed for young voices in this country."

On Tuesday, the London School of Economics Student Union's Amnesty International Unit, a student-led charity group, shared a post on Instagram saying that an event featuring Das had been "indefinitely postponed".

Saurav Das (L) speaking following the resignation of India's education minister in July

Das, a former journalist, became one of the faces of the CJP - a Gen Z-led movement that began as satire but has since emerged as a youth-led pressure group.

In July, protests spearheaded by the CJP over exam paper leaks led to the resignation of India's Education Minister Dharmendra Pradhan .

In October, CJP leaders including Das were among thousands detained by the police for trying to hold protests demanding the resignation of India's Chief Election Commissioner Gyanesh Kumar over alleged irregularities in electoral rolls. Kumar and the Election Commission have denied wrongdoing.

The same month, Das and Ashutosh Ranka, another CJP leader, were jailed for seven days after being arrested for trespassing at a banned protest site in Delhi.

Das insisted the CJP would continue protesting, but it did not want to be a political party "as of today".

"It's a long haul. We are not here for a short time. We are looking at India in the next 10 to 20 years. How do we develop India into a better country? That is the vision that we want to carry forward."

Abhijeet Dipke, the founder of CJP, wrote on X that if Das "had looted a bank or committed financial fraud", he may have been allowed to fly to London, referring to billionaire bank-loan defaulters who have left India.

There have been other instances in recent years of people being prevented from boarding flights.

In September 2025, Bhanu Tatak, an environmental and anti-dam activist from India's north-eastern Arunachal Pradesh state, was stopped from boarding a flight to Dublin.

In March 2022, Rana Ayyub, an investigative journalist and outspoken critic of Prime Minister Narendra Modi's government, was also stopped from boarding a flight to London at the Mumbai airport. She was due to speak at different events on media freedom and the targeting of journalists.

Additional reporting by Cherylann Mollan.

Google filed a patent application for an AI TV channel starring you

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ever since Google introduced its Veo 3 AI video model in March 2025, AI-generated video has started appearing in social feeds, ads, and even movies on free streaming services.

Now, Google has sketched out how AI could make the person watching TV the star of their own show. In a patent application published Nov. 19, the company describes a feature called "My Channel," which would use a viewer's watch history to generate videos for them. In one version, the system could use the viewer's likeness and voice to cast them as the lead of their very own TV show.

Patsnap, the database that hosts the filing, offers an AI-generated "technical title" that sums up the idea as a way of "enhancing user engagement."

The patent-tracking site Patentlyze first spotted the filing and shared it with 404 Media, which covered it on Nov. 23. Patent records show that Google filed the application on May 13, 2025, but the company has yet to announce a product.

One drawing in the application shows a TV menu called "John's Channel," with a crime comedy, a rom-com, a sci-fi adventure, and an action thriller. In a sample prompt, the filing tells the AI to make a video where "the user will play the protagonist." Another example takes that a step further. If a cricket fan has been watching clips about a particular player, the AI could put them in a scene with that player or show them "cheering for India in the stands." New videos could be made on request or on a schedule, such as once a week or once a month. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

To work out what to make, the system could look at the titles and genres a person watches and how long they watch them. It could also factor in the viewer's age group, the device's language setting, or its location. Those details would go into instructions for the AI about what kinds of stories to make. The application also describes an "advertising stitcher" that would insert ads into the finished video before it plays. Because even a show with an audience of one needs some sort of commercial break.

There are pieces of this idea in Google products already. Google TV Freeplay, for example, offers hundreds of free, ad-supported channels, and Google Photos can turn a picture someone selects into a short video.

Google's filing describes using photos or videos to create a digital likeness of the viewer. In one version, a TV assistant could send audio from voice commands to a server, where it could be stored "in association with the user." The system could then "synthesize the voice of the user for the dialogue of a protagonist."

The filing says viewers may have some say in what gets collected and "how that information is used." It leaves open whether someone could keep using voice commands to control the TV while opting out of having those recordings used to make a show, though.

For now, "My Channel" is still just an idea on paper. A patent examiner reportedly rejected Google's application, according to Patentlyze, for unknown reasons, and Google hasn't announced plans to launch it. The company can still seek to revise its claims or appeal. Mashable has reached out to Google for a comment.

BlackRock launches tokenized money market fund accepting stablecoins in Hong Kong

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The tokenized fund launched with approximately $100 million in assets, with Standard Chartered and Ant International participating as seed investors.

According to a Tuesday report by the South China Morning Post, the BlackRock HKD Digital Liquidity Fund launched with approximately $100 million in assets, with British banking group Standard Chartered and Ant International, a Singapore-based digital payments company spun out of China’s Ant Group, as seed investors.

The launch allows institutional investors to earn returns on their stablecoin holdings by investing directly in the fund, eliminating the need to convert their digital assets into traditional currency before investing.

The Hong Kong dollar, distinct from mainland China’s yuan, has been pegged to the US dollar since 1983.

Related: Hong Kong takes tokenized settlement to 24/7, plans stablecoin trials

BlackRock’s seed investors are also playing broader roles in the fund’s operations and adoption.

Subscriptions and redemptions can be carried out using HKDAP, a Hong Kong dollar-pegged stablecoin issued by Anchorpoint Financial, a joint venture between Standard Chartered, telecommunications company HKT and blockchain investment firm Animoca Brands. Standard Chartered also serves as the fund’s custodian, administrator, trustee and tokenization agent.

For Ant International, integrating the fund would support round-the-clock liquidity management across its global operations. According to the company’s disclosures, its payment services support more than 300 payment methods across 220 markets and process an average of over 20 million transactions daily.

BlackRock’s fund documentation shows that the fund began operations on Nov. 16, investing primarily in short-term securities issued or guaranteed by governments and high-grade bank deposits, with a net seven-day yield of 2.03% as of Tuesday. The fund may also accept tokenized deposits, while retail investors are limited to conventional Hong Kong dollars.

The fund’s launch follows HSBC’s Nov. 19 announcement that retail customers could invest in a tokenized money market fund using its Hong Kong dollar-backed stablecoin, RedCoin (RCHKD). HSBC and Anchorpoint Financial secured Hong Kong’s first two stablecoin issuer licenses in April, eight months after the city’s stablecoin regulatory framework took effect.

Everything but the Plane: Here’s How One Brand Is Bringing Glamour Back to Travel

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PS’s private terminals are the epitome of real luxury.

Imagine a trip to the airport with no lines. No lines to check in, to drop your bag, to be told your first-class ticket is the wrong first-class ticket for the first-class lounge. Now, add to that no interpersonal friction. There are no snappy desk workers, no turbo TSA employees, and no graveyard shift customs agents that give you grief.

Airlines can only solve so many issues. These are terminal problems. PS, a Los Angeles-based company operating its own luxury private terminals, is solving them. The goal is to get you as close to the charter experience as possible, all while still flying commercial. If you ask around or sit in the common areas and eavesdrop long enough, you’ll likely hear something along the lines of “I usually fly private, but when I don’t, this is what I use.”

The company has locations in Atlanta, Dallas-Fort Worth, Miami, and Los Angeles, and the next order of business is expansion to the airports its clients use most—business hubs, mostly. But it started at LAX in 2017, and that’s the perfect airport for something like this. The horseshoe design and the traffic it creates, the small terminals, and the paparazzi culture do not cater to the A-list and executive frequent fliers that use it as a home airport. PS offers a way around that with on-tarmac connection from your flight to its terminal or vice versa. A chauffeur pulls you straight off the jet bridge and into a luxury fleet vehicle; that partnership is currently with BMW. If you don’t have any checked luggage, that 7 Series can (for an additional price) drive you straight to your destination within 35 miles of the airport. So if you are sitting in row one of first class, that’s about 50 steps between you and the i7 that takes you to your hotel. Or it can take you back to the PS terminal which is not a lounge, but acts as an über private lounge experience.

It ain’t cheap: $1,300 per head one-way for the Salon (a shared bar area), $4,950 for a private suite (essentially an airport hotel room with butler and spa service, this price is all-inclusive), and $1,650 for that PS Direct drive-you-straight-home service. There are discounts and perks if you opt for yearly membership, but this is all on top of your ticket, folks.

And it can lean comically luxurious with Champagne and caviar offered every time you enter—like before my 8 a.m. departure, to which I’m not opposed, but was caught a little off guard. There’s the jet-set marketing—tagline: The “back door” to the airport—and meant-for-the-timeline branded cocktail napkins: “You’ve arrived.”

But once you accept that PS could not afford to excuse itself from the flex-driven airport exclusivity economy, you can really admire its hospitality-inspired finishes. Its raison d’être is to get you through the airport with as little friction as possible, but it stands out in its commitment to hospitality within those confines.

For example, interior design and artwork are curated. The centerpiece artworks of each location are done in collaboration with local artists, made to fit the location they are in. The crown jewel thus far is in Miami, where PS took over the old Pan Am headquarters and flight training academy. The midcentury building is a Miami landmark, with geometric thermal walls acting as the façades and this pinkish sandy and limestone-heavy concrete throughout. Argentinian-born ceramicist Nina Surel matched this with tilework water features and a ceramic mural at the center of the property. Design like this, small and mostly unnoticed in the grand scheme of things, is what turns your mind back to a golden age of travel. It’s not credit card points and moneymaking schemes—though actually, you can buy any of the non-centerpiece artworks—just beauty for the sake of beauty.

On top of that, the food and beverage program is considered and regional. The blue corn cornbread with honey and a ricotta spread that chef Kelsey Kirwin (chefs’ names are on the menus, an indication of the atmosphere) is making at the DFW location overtook my mother’s as my all-time favorite. I would consider the signature PS martini—with (guess what) caviar-stuffed olives—worthy of a place on any hot big-city cocktail menu. And all the coffees, snacks, or water options are head and shoulders above both the general population terminals and the litany of airport lounges within them.

As far as service, yes, an agent handles your party’s check-in and keeps you abreast of delays so you don’t have to open an app—but that’s to be expected. It’s more in the interim. Servers don’t hover, they listen, and they’re backed by an extensive behind-the-scenes operation: Mention you prefer coffee, not bubbles, before a morning flight? A note is made on your profile for the next time you visit. Only ever ask for sparkling water when you fly out of LAX? They’ll know that in Atlanta. The entire operation seems to run on Guidara’s Unreasonable Hospitality, the same way all modern restaurants do. If you’re leaving Miami and mention to a business partner that you forgot to try a Cubano (on menu) and a cafecito (off menu), both will likely be delivered to your table, compliments of the house.

But every time I start to get wowed by the service details, I remember the price point and the level of clientele. That should be expected.

Standard terminals are airside, post-security, where everything (even a fancy and expensive airline lounge) is working within those confines. Since PS operates landside, with security the last stage of the terminal experience, it has the flexibility to be more hospitable to guests. For example, if you book a private suite, you can fly in, use it for a business meeting, and fly out. Or, your parents can accompany the family to departure, hang out in the suite, and squeeze every last second with the grandkids out of your visit. It is an airport experience actually designed around the ways we interact and what matters to us when we travel. That, more than the Champagne and caviar, is the definition of luxury.

Rep. Raskin probes Trump Jr.’s firm’s investments into Polymarket and Juul over ‘suspiciously well-timed government favors’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Rep. Raskin is set to lead the House Judiciary Committee next year after Democrats took both the House and Senate in the midterm elections.

Rep. Jamie Raskin, who is poised to lead a key committee next year, has expanded his investigation into Donald Trump Jr.’s venture capital firm, probing potential links between investments in companies such as Polymarket and what he has called "suspiciously well-timed government favors."

On Monday, the Maryland Democrat sent a letter to AI-focused manufacturing company Hadrian Automation, e-cigarette company Juul Labs, prediction markets platform Polymarket and online firearms retailer GrabAGun over their ties to Trump's 1789 Capital and whether President Donald Trump's son and business partners helped influence regulatory decisions or use certain knowledge to inform their investment decisions.

"The House Judiciary Committee is committed to ensuring that public officials and their families cannot profit from and exploit inside government information and insider access. The American people demand and expect nothing less," Raskin said on Tuesday.

Raskin is set to lead the House Judiciary Committee next year after Democrats took both the House and Senate in the midterm elections. Raskin first began probing 1789 Capital in August. He has also criticized President Trump's ties to the crypto industry and said he had "turned the Oval Office into the world’s most corrupt crypto startup operation."

In his letter to Polymarket on Monday, Raskin noted the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's previous settlement with Polymarket after the platform allegedly offered illicit binary options contracts and, as part of that agreement, said it would pay a $1.4 million fine, wind down its non-compliant markets and take preventive measures to block U.S. users. In July, the CFTC and the Justice Department dropped that probe last year.

Polymarket then secured an investment from 1789 Capital about a month afterwards.

"Only after the Trump Administration had cleared the way for Polymarket to compete in the U.S. market did the company disclose that 1789 Capital reportedly invested tens of millions of dollars in the company," Raskin said.

Polymarket did not respond to a request for comment. Last week, Donald Trump Jr. pushed back against conflicts of interest at an Axios event, saying "the notion that I should somehow sit in a corner, suck my thumb, and wait, you know, 8 years, in this case, 12 years, 'til we're out of the political game, it's not real."

Lawmakers and policymakers at the state level have also raised concerns about other aspects of prediction markets, including insider trading and whether states or the CFTC should regulate the platforms. On Tuesday, New York City Council Speaker Julie Menin requested that prediction market executives appear before a council oversight hearing next month following an investigation alleging platforms are targeting younger people with "deceptive, predatory marketing practices."

"As a mom and consumer protection attorney, I believe we must end these companies’ predatory marketing tactics," Menin said. 'The City Council will hold a hearing to consider first-in-the-nation regulations of these practices, and we expect these companies to testify.”

Kalshi makes “difficult decision” to halt Utah trading after losing court fights

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kalshi’s pause in Utah draws cheers as critics bash ads and boo insider trading.

Kalshi made the “difficult decision” to pause operations in Utah after losing a court fight to block the state’s ongoing enforcement actions under Utah gambling laws, Kalshi spokesperson Jacki McGavick said Monday.

It seems Kalshi’s legal troubles in Utah have become too much for the prediction market platform to gamble on continued operations there. In Utah, several lawsuits are still playing out, and Kalshi also lost a bid to persuade a federal appeals court to block those cases until the appeal is resolved.

Kalshi also wasn’t saved by an emergency motion from the Commodity Futures Trading Commission—which argues that the CFTC has exclusive jurisdiction over prediction market trading. At this time, the federal district court has not granted that motion to block Utah’s enforcement actions, either.

“This is an unfortunate outcome for the more than 177,000 Utahns who use Kalshi,” McGavick said. “We will continue to fight this in court, and we look forward to returning to Utah once our legal position is vindicated.”

Utah’s attorney general, Derek Brown, had filed both civil and criminal charges against Kalshi, as he defended the state’s 130-year ban on gambling.

In a press release discussing the initial lawsuit, he accused Kalshi of running “an online casino” that violates the state’s constitution. “Not only do our state laws ban gambling but Utah is one of only two states where gambling is also prohibited under the state constitution,” Brown said. “For 130 years, Utah has been clear: gambling has no place here, and we will enforce our laws.”

Late Monday, a major Utah area news outlet, KSL.com, reported that Brown was prepared to jail Kalshi’s co-founders, who had been summoned to be booked on 14 charges on December 5. He defended those plans, saying, “There is a question I have seen from people asking, ‘Well, do we modify this booking process if someone is a very wealthy and well-known tech billionaire?’ and the answer is ‘No, the process is the process; we don’t modify it based on who you are.”

However, on Tuesday, Utah decided to drop the criminal charges against Kalshi, McGavick confirmed to Ars. In a statement on X, Kalshi general counsel, Yaakov Roth, said that “we are pleased that Utah has chosen to dismiss criminal charges against Kalshi, while proceeding with civil litigation. Good faith jurisdictional disagreements should never be criminalized. We stand ready to work with State AGs on reasonable interim measures until courts resolve the dispute over who regulates prediction markets.”

In a press release, Brown explained that the charges were dropped specifically due to the pause.

“Kalshi said it would geofence Utah, which blocks access to its site in the state,” the press release said. “The company also committed to monitor for Utah-based IP addresses trying to reach its site. The Office then reevaluated its position and moved to dismiss the criminal case against the company and the individual defendants.”

Brown threatened that the charges would be raised again if Kalshi tried to renew operations.

“Utah does not allow gambling, and prediction markets are gambling,” Brown said. Kalshi has pulled out of Utah, and if it returns, so will the charges. Every company in this space should take note. If they promote, support, or enable gambling, we will hold them accountable.”

Discussion of the Kalshi pause in a Utah sub-Reddit found some Redditors skeptical of the state’s cases as potentially overreaching.

However, many top commenters seemed pleased with the enforcement efforts, suggesting that Utah may have bipartisan support among some residents. One commenter cheered on the attorney general’s efforts as “the one item in the Utah GOP’s agenda I can get behind,” while another agreed that “prediction markets are incredibly toxic and are manipulated against ordinary people.”

Many complained about Kalshi ads flooding their social media feeds. Some suggested the state should focus on banning advertising of prediction markets, while potentially still allowing adults to trade on event contracts if they want to  throw their “money away.”

Fears of insider trading were also raised. One comment laid out why prediction markets seem suspiciously designed to promote insider trading and other market manipulation:

“I’m pretty libertarian and hate banning things. But gambling and prediction markets are a scourge. Even the term ‘prediction market’ is a misnomer. Because ultimately when people with big dollars get involved it creates a financial incentive for them to make fucked up things happen in the real world. Think about it, decades ago we understood that sports betting was a problem because it corrupted sports—something that ultimately is pretty meaningless. Then we got all these people betting on the stock market with puts and it created huge incentives to cause havoc with markets. Now if people are betting big money on politics and wars and everything else, it just means people with influence can make money by making improbable/unexpected things happen.”

One commenter joked, “I wonder how Kashi would have reacted if the judge in charge of their case had ‘traded’ $500,000 on the event of them losing the case. Not a problem at all, right?”

On the same day Kalshi announced its temporary pause in Utah, a judge rejected a request from New York’s attorney general, Letitia James, to immediately shut down Kalshi in that state, Courthouse News reported.

New York Supreme Court Justice Melissa Crane said she was “uncomfortable” granting the temporary restraining order so early in the case because “there does seem to be an indication that there would be market disruption that I’m concerned about.” Kalshi attorney Grant Mainland described it a little differently, claiming that a sudden halt would cause a “market emergency.”

“I think this is the ultimate relief in the case, which is improper for me to do without full briefing,” Crane said. Instead of granting James’ request, she recommended that Kalshi take steps to address the state’s top concerns by considering “safeguards for users between 18 and 20,” who are under the state’s legal gambling age, Courthouse News reported.

According to Kalshi’s estimates, New York’s Kalshi user base (1.5 million) is much larger than Utah’s, so the market disruption from the Utah shutdown may not have carried the same dire stakes for Kalshi or its broader user base.

The CFTC has sought to intervene in that case, too, and it seems clear that Kalshi expects the questions of who can regulate prediction markets and what trading qualifies as a “bet” to reach the Supreme Court. Kalshi’s spokesperson did not respond to Ars’ questions on how the Utah pause broadly impacted users or the platform.

So far, “three federal appeals courts have ruled that states can regulate prediction markets, while a fourth found they cannot,” Courthouse News reported. But despite the current balance tipping toward allowing states to regulate prediction markets, Kalshi appears confident that the Supreme Court will resolve the disparate court rulings in its favor.

In a lawsuit filed by a disgruntled Kalshi user, Kalshi argued that it would waste lower courts’ time to continue litigating these cases and that no case should be resolved until the Supreme Court weighs in.

In that case, Kalshi user Scott Bright alleged that he lost more than $300,000 on sports bets that were technically illegal in Washington.

According to Kalshi, all of Bright’s claims rest “on the premise that state gambling laws reach KalshiEX’s federally regulated exchange—the question now before the Supreme Court. Plaintiff puts the cart before the horse; Defendants ask only that the Court let that threshold question be resolved first.”

The court has yet to address Kalshi’s request to stay the case, but Bright has argued that Kalshi is trying to “sideline a case governed by binding Ninth Circuit precedent so that they can wait for a Supreme Court decision that may never come.”

Kalshi did not respond to Ars’ request for a statement on Bright’s lawsuit. In their court filing, they argued that “a stay will let the Supreme Court address an issue that could dispose of every claim, will not meaningfully harm Plaintiff, and will spare Defendants and the Court litigation under a framework that may soon be displaced.”

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Chatting With People In GTA Online About Why They’re Not Playing GTA 6: ‘$80 Is Too Much For Me, Bro’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6 is the biggest video game on console right now. Everyone in my life, even people who don’t normally play these kinds of games, is booting up GTA 6 and spending hours exploring Vice City. But what about the people still playing GTA Online? What do they think of the newest entry which, for the time being, remains single-player? I dropped back into Rockstar’s existing multiplayer juggernaut a few days after GTA 6‘s launch to check out the vibes and ask people how they were feeling. The answers varied, but a surprisingly common response was that they were still playing GTA Online because they just couldn’t afford to buy GTA 6 right now.

GTA 5‘s live-service platform is still be very popular in 2026, but when I returned recently following the launch of GTA 6, it was definitely emptier than usual. I hopped into different sessions looking for players who were actually chatting on the mic, and found most lobbies to not be anywhere near close to full. In fact, many had only a few players hanging out in lobbies that support up to 30. I also ended up back in the same sessions again and again, which is not something that usually happens in GTA Online. And the sessions I landed in were filled with mostly low-level players and kids. A lot of the high-level kings and queens of GTA Online were missing. I assume they were chilling in Vice City.

One of the first people I spoke to was one of these GTA Online kids. They sounded no older than 12, and when I asked if they had played GTA 6, they told me no. I asked why and was told: “It sucks, bro.” I asked for more details, pretending I had yet to play it and was curious about it.

“It just sucks,” said the kid as he blew up a rival CEO. “I watched my cousin play it, bro, it just sucks. Likeâ¦it has good graphics, but GTA Online is more fun. It just sucks. That’s all I’m going to say about it, bro.â

Another player, who sounded a bit older, replied that “GTA 6 was shit” and that all the critics got “paid to glaze it.” Then he blew up the kid who said it sucked, and they started yelling at each other. At one point, the younger kid almost sounded like they were crying. It was awkward. Later, I ended up back in that session, and the kid who may have been crying was still very mad. Nobody else was talking in that session, so I hopped to a new one.

Eventually, I found a high-level player who was happy to chat. According to him, he desperately wants to play GTA 6, but money is tight.

“I gotta wait until I get paid again, dude,” the player told me over voice chat. “Iâve been waiting years…I can wait longer. I’m pretending I’m in GTA 6 right now. This chick next to me is Lucia. Or maybe Mandy!Â  Well, not as hot as her.” He laughed, and I asked if he’d been watching videos of GTA 6 while waiting to get paid and actually play it.Â

“Oh yeah, I’ve seen videos. Dude, it looks phenomenal. I can’t wait, man. But like, until then I’m here in GTA Online pretending it’s Vice City.” More laughter.

GTA Online is a community of players who will kill you in a heartbeat with a giant tank or flying car, but won’t ever say a single word to you before, during, or after. Shy little murderers. It’s been like this for years. So as I hopped from session to session, I also texted players via the in-game phone. It’s clunky, but I did get a few replies from people more willing to chat via text.

One person I texted, a level 7 player, confirmed that they just started playing GTA Online that very night. When I asked about GTA 6, they said they wanted to play, but it costs too much. So instead, the game’s historic launch inspired them to go back and finally give GTA Online a try. They had owned GTA 5 for a bit now and decided to kill time waiting for GTA 6 by making money in GTA Online. Another person I texted in-game simply told me GTA 6 looked boring and dumb. They apparently had no interest in playing it. Another player simply texted back, “$80 is too much for me, bro.”

In a different session, I found a gamer who claimed that everything they’d seen about GTA 6 made the game look “bad.”

“I don’t know, dude, seems boring,” said the player who sounded around my age. “Like I don’t wanna buy flowers for a girl and fix shit, bro. I wanna kill people and steal shit.” A moment later, that player yelled in anger as he was blown up by someone who was running around killing anyone they spotted with a flying motorcycle.Â

As I wrapped up my time back in GTA Online, I heard more kids yelling at each other about stealing drugs or something. In the background, I heard one kid yell out that he was going to invite someone else. The kid he was going to invite began screaming and crying in the background. “I don’t want to play GTA 5 ONLINE!”

Another person joked that he must want to play GTA 6 instead. And then he added: “My parents won’t buy GTA 6 for me because it’s, like, filled with boobs and shit.”

After playing GTA Online again and chatting with more people over voice and text, what I learned is that while some players are staying away from GTA 6 because of silly complaints about it being woke, the vast majority of players either don’t care about that or have no idea some online are complaining about that crap. Instead, the real reason most of them aren’t playing GTA 6 is simply that they don’t have the money to buy it right now. And many of them really want to play it.Â  That sucks for them, but it’s good news for Take-Two and Rockstar. GTA 6 is already selling incredibly well, and if my time in GTA Online has revealed one thing, it’s that even more people are going to be buying it soon once they can afford it.

UK warns AI-driven cyberattacks are exposing years of weak security

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The U.K. government’s cybersecurity unit says it has seen a “marked rise” in AI-driven cyberattacks in recent months, as hackers increasingly rely on autonomous hacking tools to identify and exploit simple weaknesses to break into systems and networks.

The U.K. National Cybersecurity Center (NCSC) said it has seen several cyber incidents since September involving autonomous hacking tools, such as penetration testing tools used by defenders to stress-test their cybersecurity defenses. These incidents have caused intrusions at organizations and resulted in downtime, the unit said in a blog post.

The NCSC says that while none of the vulnerabilities being exploited are new, the speed at which these AI tools are able to carry out simple hacks has changed.

The rise in AI-related cyberattacks coincides with the broader release earlier this year of open source autonomous penetration testing tools like Artex and Cairn, among others, which anyone can download and use at no cost. The U.S. Federal Bureau of Investigation recently warned that malicious hackers are using AI penetration testing tools to carry out cyberattacks at unprecedented speeds.

While cyberattacks and intrusions are on the rise, the message from London is that many of these attacks are preventable with basic cybersecurity defenses and best practices.

“Notably, the issues being exploited currently are not novel memory corruption vulnerabilities (or even known but unpatched vulnerabilities), but unmitigated weaknesses that are often the result of years of technical debt that organisations have accumulated,” the NCSC wrote.

The unit said that agentic hacking tools often look for exposed or internet-facing systems, then quickly run through lists of weak or easy-to-guess passwords or abuse API endpoints often used by developers to access data.

The NCSC said that AI “does not make established cyber security controls obsolete,” but rather that “building strong foundations has never mattered more.”

The unit added that AI has made it easier to find vulnerabilities and faster to exploit them, warning that weaknesses that have gone unnoticed for years can now be found in minutes.

Bluetooth trackers have never been cheaper

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you've been thinking about picking up a Bluetooth tracker to keep tabs on your keys, luggage, or other valuables, now is probably the best time to get one. Apple launched its second-generation AirTag 2 earlier this year, but the tracker is already down to its lowest price yet for Black Friday. You can now grab a single Apple AirTag for just $18 at Amazon, down from its usual $29 price.

And if you're an Android user, there's a pretty good deal for you as well. The Moto Tag 2, which offers many of the same features as Apple's AirTag, is currently down to just $19 at Amazon.

Apple's latest AirTag features an improved Precision Finding range, a 50% louder speaker, and up to a year of battery life. It works seamlessly with Apple's Find My network and is now down to just $18 (38% off).

The second-generation AirTag offers up to 1.5 times the Precision Finding range of its predecessor. Apple has also upgraded the built-in speaker, making it 50% louder than the original AirTag, and features a new, more distinctive chime. this makes it a lot easier to locate your belongings when they're nearby.

One of the biggest reasons to get an AirTag over most other Apple Find My-compatible trackers, though, is Precision Finding. While other key finders and trackers also let you locate your belongings through the Find My app warn you if you leave something behind, the AirTag takes things a step further with its ultra-wideband (UWB) technology.

If you've misplaced your keys somewhere around the house, for example, you can simply open the Find My app on a compatible iPhone, and it'll show you exactly which direction to walk in and how far away your AirTag is — rather than just relying on a sound to locate it.

And if you're planning to put AirTags on multiple belongings, there's also a deal on the AirTag four-pack, which is currently down to $79 from its usual $99 price. That said, at $18 apiece, buying four individual AirTags actually works out slightly cheaper.

If you're on the Android side of things, the Moto Tag 2 is arguably one of the best alternatives to Apple's AirTag. Much like Apple's AirTag, the Moto Tag 2 features ultra-wideband technology, which means you can use precision finding on compatible Android phones to pinpoint the location of your items.

It also works with Google's Find Hub network, so you can locate your items even when they're outside your phone's Bluetooth range.

The Moto Tag 2 brings an AirTag-like tracking experience to Android with UWB Precision Finding, Google's Find Hub integration, over 600 days of battery life, and IP68 water resistance. It's currently down to just $19 from $30.

But there are a couple of things the Moto Tag 2 does even better than Apple's AirTag. For starters, Motorola claims over 600 days of battery life, which is considerably longer than the year of battery life Apple promises. You can also use the button on the Moto Tag 2 as a remote camera shutter, which is particularly handy for taking group photos or selfies.

You also get features like left-behind notifications and an IP68 dust- and water-resistance rating, so you don't have to worry too much about attaching it to your backpack or other belongings you carry outdoors.

On the whole, I think $20 is a pretty reasonable amount to spend on something that could save you a lot of trouble if you ever lose your keys or luggage. And if you've been holding off on buying an item tracker for your phone, now's a pretty good time to get one, regardless of whether you use an iPhone or an Android phone.

Elizabeth Holmes’ Ex-Assistant Speaks Out on ‘You Can See Everything’ and Amanda Seyfried’s ‘Valid’ Performance: Holmes ‘Softened a Lot in the Film’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For Aria Marinelli, “You Can See Everything” hit a little close to home.

Marinelli, who worked as Elizabeth Holmes’ assistant in 2009, during the early stages of Theranos, spoke about watching her former boss in the documentary in an interview with Warner Bailey on “AvA Live.”

“My initial reaction was I did not want to see it,” Marinelli said of the Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim film. But then, eventually, “I acquiesced and went out of sheer morbid curiosity, and I came out of there just feeling really contemplative. I mean, she’s a real human that I interacted with, and to see her up there going to jail with children was really emotionally charged for me, as someone who does have a kid as well.” Related Stories ‘Ginny & Georgia’ Sets Season 4 Release Date, Drops First Look Photos Russell Brand Documentary From 'Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story' Producers Set at Netflix Days After Rape Sentencing

When “You Can See Everything” surprise premiered at Telluride and became one of the buzziest films of the year, Marinelli, who has rarely spoken about her time at Theranos 17 years ago, realized, “I have a voice in this too. [Holmes] doesn’t get to be the one to just come back on the scene and control the narrative.”

Marinelli called the film a “crazy, wonderful, thoughtful, strategic piece,” and praised Fielder’s ability to portray Holmes as she truly is. “I do think he’s done it justice.”

Compared to her time running the fraudulent blood-testing startup, “She seems like she’s softened a lot in the film,” Marinelli said of Holmes. “But she still seems like she really wants to win.”

Amanda Seyfried plays a big part in “You Can See Everything,” reprising her role as Holmes in reenactments with Fielder after the Theranos founder went to prison. One of the tension points in the three-hour A24 film is Seyfried’s portrayal of Holmes in the hit Hulu miniseries “The Dropout.” Billy Evans, Holmes’ romantic partner, clashes with Seyfried in the movie as he accuses her (and everyone else who worked on the series) of peddling lies about Holmes for the sake of entertainment.

Marinelli, who knew Holmes intimately in 2009, said watching “The Dropout” was “remarkable” because “I can vouch for a lot of that.” (As an example: “I remember she listened to rap music really loud.”)

Asked if she thought Seyfried’s depiction of Holmes was “fair,” Marinelli said, “Yeah, I do, because a lot of the things I saw were measured and valid. Obviously they’re dramatized, because it needs to keep people’s interest. But there was nothing I saw that seemed out of sorts with what I knew to be true.”

Holmes is currently serving a 10-year prison sentence and is expected to be released in 2030. In the film, she and Evans tell Fielder they are working on a new biotech startup, continuing the progress of Theranos.

As to whether Holmes deserves a second chance, Marinelli said, “I think people are complex, and we always deserve second chances. It might not be the best idea to go into the same field where you’ve already burned all the bridges, very publicly. It’s a complex question, and not for me to answer, because she needs to decide the direction her life takes after something like that. I can’t imagine what it must feel like to reset, especially if she truly believes she was following her dream. But yeah, I think we all deserve a second chance.”

Bailey is the founder of Assistants vs. Agents, a digital media startup geared toward Hollywood’s labor class. On “AvA Live,” he interviews entertainment industry insiders and former assistants sharing advice for the next generation of leaders. Marinelli now works as the vice president of fan experience strategy at Arena One.

Premiership side look to replicate Champions League club’s progress plan as ‘explosive’ stat shows clear need

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Alfred Johansson insists Motherwell’s latest accounts show the club are trending upwards - with Norway’s Bodo Glimt a key reference point.

The Premiership side have reported a profit of £2.3M, which rises to £3.1M when excluding depreciation and finance costs, for the 2025/26 financial year. A record of £5M was generated from player trading while former manager Jens Berthel Askou achieved a fourth place finish in last term’s top flight.

Current manager Johansson’s work was largely in Scandinavia before travelling to Motherwell. He watched Bodo’s rise from lowly divisions to Champions League competitors and feels that shows what a step-by-step approach can do for a club’s long term aspirations. Former Rosenborg gaffer Johansson said: “I can't really use that to ask for more time in terms of performing but definitely, it's evidence of what the club has been doing for the past 18 months at least, that it’s something that is working.

“So that alignment in the club, that philosophy, and that strategy, like we do things like this, and the connection I feel between people inside here and the offices, that everyone wants to pull together in that direction. That is what gives you output all the time.

“This is a club that the budget is very small compared with some of the teams that we want to compete with. That was the same for Bodo/Glimt 10 years ago. Their budget was so small, they went down to the 2nd division in Norway, now they are playing Champions League. You have to build stone by stone, and that's what Motherwell are trying to do, and that's what I feel every day inside these walls.”

Motherwell’s long-term goals are also set to be boosted by a training base being built at nearby Dalziel Park where they currently undergo preparation for matches. Johansson is keen to see the club build on its ability to grasp marginal gains. He explained: ”You don't have that much time in football nowadays. I get asked in the press conference about patience and everything is related to time.

“That means that you have to make the most out of your time. Maybe before 30 years ago you had to optimise every week. Now you have to optimise every hour in top football. That's down to recovery of players, work with their bodies, and you need facilities to be able to facilitate that work. So that's one part of it.

“That you need to add the facilities for the players to be able to do the work that's needed to play every few days and with that intensity that. Now I saw that from all the top leagues in Europe, that the Scottish Premiership was the second in the world in explosive accelerations, for instance.

“That's one of the more demanding things that you can put the body through. So you need to be able to react. You need to recover from that between matches and trainings, and so on. So you need the facilities for that. We don't have that much time. Right now we are driving back and forth to Dalziel Park. I could maybe take five minutes in my office with a player or on the pitch talking to a player. All of this is optimising time and I think it's essential today.”

Dundee boss Steven Pressley spoke last week of the lack of national performance centre in Scotland, like Sweden are building in Johansson’s homeland. He added: “I definitely see the point of that's needed. I mean, Denmark, they've been working also now for some years.

“They have a plan now in place for how to build that centre, Sweden the same. Uh, we know that other countries, they did it 20 years ago, like like the Belgians and the Portuguese FA, they did it. They built a new facility after the Euros. So, of course, it's there to push things and it's needed.”
