Eric Trump’s Bitcoin miner surges 17% after $150 million deal to avoid selling BTC

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The financing gives American Bitcoin another source of liquidity while competitors increasingly sell Bitcoin to finance data-center expansion.

American Bitcoin secured a $150 million Bitcoin-backed credit facility as other listed miners sell reserves to finance AI infrastructure.

The Eric Trump-linked company disclosed the revolving credit agreement with FalconX in a Nov. 23 SEC filing, giving it access to capital without selling Bitcoin or issuing additional shares.

Data from Yahoo Finance showed that American Bitcoin shares climbed as high as $4.38 during Monday's trading before closing 16.3% higher at $4.07. Despite the rebound, the stock remains approximately 84% below its level at the start of the year.

The financing comes as the company continues expanding its Bitcoin holdings, which recently surpassed 8,500 BTC.

American Bitcoin said the accumulation has moved it from 15th to 13th among publicly traded corporate Bitcoin holders worldwide.

The company combines Bitcoin mining with direct accumulation, making the preservation of its reserves central to its long-term strategy.

“This $150 million facility provides American Bitcoin with non-dilutive capital to support our continued operations and growth initiatives.”

The approach contrasts with a broader shift across the mining industry.

Blockchain analytics firm Glassnode reported that publicly listed miners in its tracked group had reduced their combined Bitcoin reserves by nearly half since January. The firm attributed the decline to miners increasingly using Bitcoin holdings to finance their expansion into artificial intelligence data centers.

For years, many publicly traded miners retained their mined Bitcoin and sometimes purchased additional coins. That strategy has increasingly given way to asset sales as operators redirect capital toward AI computing infrastructure.

American Bitcoin's arrangement offers another financing route, allowing it to retain ownership of its Bitcoin while pledging some holdings as collateral for dollar liquidity.

The agreement allows American Bitcoin's subsidiary to borrow up to $150 million from FalconX Charlie, secured by Bitcoin held at Anchorage Digital Bank.

According to the SEC filing, borrowings carry a 5.95% annual loan fee, alongside commitment fees on unused capacity. The facility matures in November 2028, with a possible three-year extension.

However, preserving Bitcoin through collateralized borrowing introduces exposure to forced asset sales during market downturns.

The agreement requires pledged Bitcoin to cover at least 143% of outstanding principal when funds are drawn. If that ratio falls below 130%, FalconX can demand additional Bitcoin or partial repayment to restore the collateral requirement.

American Bitcoin’s subsidiary had drawn nothing on the $150 million FalconX line as of the Nov. 23 filing. These thresholds govern draws and outstanding loans.

The 143% ratio serves as the draw minimum and the target after a margin-call top-up or partial collateral release.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

Markers are spaced proportionately on each axis using the contractual ratio of pledged bitcoin value to total outstanding principal. Thresholds come from Item 1.01, the master agreement and the signed term sheet.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

A decline below 105% can trigger default and potentially allow the lender to liquidate pledged assets under the agreement's conditions.

At the minimum initial collateral ratio, a Bitcoin price decline of about 9.1% would trigger a margin call if the company borrowed and neither added collateral nor repaid debt.

American Bitcoin already had 3,090 BTC pledged for mining equipment purchases as of Sept. 30, according to its quarterly filing.

The new facility could let it access more capital while maintaining its Bitcoin position, but borrowing would also put additional holdings behind collateral obligations.

Still, the arrangement leaves American Bitcoin on a different path from miners selling reserves to fund AI infrastructure, with its ability to retain those coins increasingly tied to BTC's market value and how much it ultimately borrows.

How To Get Your Free Snom Ornament From The Pokemon Center

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Between games, plushes, and trading cards, being a Pokemon fan can get pretty pricey. That’s why it’s always welcome when The Pokemon Company gives its fans something for free, and if you’ve got more merch to buy heading into the holidays, then there might be a new ornament waiting for you when you head to the Pokemon Center checkout.

The promotion kicked off on November 23, 2026, which means it’s live right now, and it’ll bag you a free Snom ornament to hang from your Christmas tree - or wherever else you might want to hang it - provided you meet all of the criteria for a qualifying purchase.

The first thing you need to do is make sure you try to get your Snom before it’s too late. The promotion will run until 11:59 pm PST on December 25, 2026, so you have plenty of time. However, that’s only if Pokemon doesn’t run out of Snom ornaments. This is a “while stocks last” situation, and there’s no indication of just how many Snom Pokemon has to go around.

Don't hang around if you missed them the first time. They'll be gone again before you know it.

As long as the deadline hasn’t passed and Pokemon still has them in stock, you will qualify for a free Snom ornament when you spend $50 or more in a single transaction at the Pokemon Center. The only caveat is pre-order and digital products don’t qualify, so no buying stuff that isn’t out yet, and digital versions of games won’t count towards your $50 total.

There is no code to enter at checkout. The Snom ornament should simply be added to your cart when you go to pay and be shipped along with the rest of your order. Just make sure it’s there before you actually check out. The ornaments are limited to one per customer, meaning you won’t get two if you spend over $100, and if you want a refund on anything in your order, the ornament has to be returned along with it.

Pokemon’s holiday collection is now live, so you can shop from that to get your free ornament, and you can also buy all of Pokemon’s Lego sets so far through the Pokemon Center. That includes the Iconic Trainer Moments Poke Ball set which has Red & Blue easter eggs hiding inside. You can get that set for less on Amazon right now, but buying it there means you won’t get your Snom.

Only a week after its best set yet was released, details about three more Pokemon Lego sets have leaked.

GTA 6 Has Recaptured the Beautifully Borked Energy of Skyrim

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6 is a towering technological achievement. It is an open-world game made up of criss-crossing emergent systems that make it feel like life is unfolding all around you, whether you’re there to see it or not. It’s not easy to pull off something so complex — and players are learning that in real time. A few days into its lifespan, players are already finding a host of quirks and bugs that are turning the hyperrealistic GTA 6 into a slapstick comedy show.

While my initial playthrough largely went off without a hitch, I’ve begun to see the seams for myself as I get more experimental with my post-game play. And while I’m sure that many of the bugs I’ve experienced will be patched out over time, I’m secretly hoping Rockstar leaves a few in. In its current state, GTA 6 has all the imperfect charm of an Elder Scrolls game — with 1000% more exploding trucks.

There are two kinds of technical eccentricities you’re bound to come across in GTA 6. The first are the strange, hilarious things that happen when complex systems collide. These moments happen where enemy AI meets game logic and physics in some unexpected way. The best example of this so far can be seen in a viral clip where Lucia and Jason run up to a drawbridge that is about to lower. The AI-controlled Jason attempts to vault over a lowered barrier arm… at the exact moment that it’s lifting. It flings the poor guy into the air, like a scene out of a Wile E. Coyote toon. Another personal favorite clip I’ve seen involves an NPC backing up his car, and the open passenger side door knocking an unaware Jason flat on his ass.

These aren’t bugs; it’s the game working exactly as intended, on some level. Slapstick moments born from systems that don’t revolve solely around the player create the sense that accidents can and do happen in Leonida. Drive around long enough and you’re bound to see something like a car taking a turn too wide and accidentally knocking down an entire traffic light. These little moments turn GTA 6 into an unpredictable comedy of emergent errors.

That’s the same energy that once drew me into Bethesda’s RPGs. Perhaps my favorite game clip of all time is a video from Oblivion of an NPC saying “Farewell!” before getting crushed by a pressure plate, only for another NPC to approach his dead body and say “May he rest in peace” as he is also crushed to death. It’s a hysterical collision of systems that results in comedic gold that you couldn't script that well if you tried. “This is the most Oblivion video of all time,” a YouTube comment reads. “A perfect representation of what we love about the game.” I’m already feeling the same way about GTA 6 when I see clips of Jason accidentally eating shit.

Then there are the unintended accidents, which makes GTA 6 even more like an Elder Scrolls game. Players are already discovering a host of weird bugs that cause cars to explode, boats to go flying down the street, and alligators to ascend into the sky. (You can watch some great ones via Highlight Reel.) I’ve experienced some of these for myself, and they are often hilarious. At one point, I lightly bumped into the back of a truck with my car to try and get it to move out of my way. The physics went haywire, and suddenly I was treated to a slow-motion crash sequence as both cars launched straight into the air. That’s a common quirk in GTA 6 right now, and it’s making for a lot of gut-busting clips.

In another game, bugs like this could be a major problem. You don’t want to spend three hours struggling through a Dark Souls fight only to die to a geometry glitch. But in GTA 6, it’s easy to laugh them off. (At least now that GTA 6’s unreliable autosave has been tweaked via the game’s first patch.) The sandbox joy of the entire GTA series has always come from finding the silly ways you can get yourself killed while trying to push a game to its technical limits. I’m usually delighted when I trigger a malfunction, because it feels like I’ve thoroughly prodded the simulation. The reward is getting to see the underside of the physics system as Jason goes flying through the air.

One of my favorite examples of this thus far comes from a video of a train getting caught on a rising bridge. The train begins wiggling around like a snake as screaming passengers rain down from it. A Reddit post sharing the video prompted one commenter to wonder if that means that trains now have their own physics, and thus can be derailed. You can see an experiment forming in real time as a glitch revealed something about how GTA 6 was built. There’s a joy in embracing the mechanical nature of games when the results are harmless and hilarious.

“People can shit on glitches all they want, but random shit like this is what makes gaming special,” one comment on the Reddit thread reads. “It’s a video game, I don’t expect them to be perfect. Plus how is it bad when it brings so much entertainment?”
